Ἐπεὶ καὶ ἔπρεπεν αὐτῷ, καὶ ὠφείλετο, ἀνθρώπῳ φιλανθρωπίᾳ γινομένῳ δι᾿ ἀνθρώπων ἀπάντων ἐκλύτρωσιν, ἐκ μόνης Παρθένου τὸν τοκετὸν ἐπιδείξασθαι, ἡδονῆς ἐκτὸς γαμικῆς καὶ συμβάσεως ποιησαμένῳ τὴν ἐκ σοῦ περιφανῆ καὶ σωτήριον σάρκωσιν.

Μʹ.

Having heard these things from her who would truly become Mother of God, the archangel, the herald of the good tidings, marvelling at her answer and her understanding, answered: Excellently indeed and most wisely, O Virgin Mother of God, hast thou discoursed of how of old women 3269who were barren before became mothers of children afterwards, and in a manner forced their own nature, and bore children beyond nature, God having compassion, and the Master and Creator and Fashioner of nature having pity. For where God nods assent, the boundaries of nature are idle.

Just as those women, then, God ordaining it, conquered the passions of their own nature, and, having loosed great passions of nature, brought forth fruit of the womb; so thou too, who didst spring from barren Anna, and didst conquer the nature of her who bore thee, being a pure virgin, and remaining a virgin afterwards, shalt bear paradoxically a son without seed and without ploughing, he himself who is born commanding thee. For he is also Lord of nature, being its Creator and ruler. For he is God incomprehensible, even if as man he shall be born of thee, the Virgin. Since it also befitted him, and was owed, becoming man in love for man for the redemption of all men, to show forth his childbirth from a Virgin alone, having made the conspicuous and saving incarnation from thee apart from marriage pleasure and union.

40. But if the fact that a virgin pure and undefiled has not yet till this day borne, nor has a mother of a seedless child ever been proclaimed, makes thy mind doubtful, and thou seekest to learn when a virgin has given birth; I too ask thee and inquire, when, then, did God become man? Or when did God accept to be incarnate? Or when has God come to be man? Or when was God conceived without seed? Or when did God endure child-bearing? Or when did God show forth a fleshly birth from a woman? Or when did God suck at women's breasts? Or when did God give forth an infant's wailing? Or when did God receive growth in stature? Or when has God lived as a citizen with us men? Or when did God die for men? Or when has God had the nailing of a cross? Or when was God pierced in the side with a lance? Or when did God undergo a three-day burial?

Εἰ δὲ ὅτι παρθένος καθαρὰ καὶ ἀμόλυντος οὔπω μέχρι καὶ τήμερον τέτοκεν, οὐδὲ μήτηρ ἀσπόρου ποτὲ παιδὸς ἀνηγόρευται, ἀμφίβολόν σου ποιεῖ τὴν διάνοιαν, καὶ μαθεῖν ἐπιζητεῖς, πότε παρθένος γεγέννηκεν· κἀγώ σε διερωτῶ καὶ πυνθάνομαι, πότε γὰρ Θεὸς ἐνηνθρώπησεν· ἢ πότε σαρκωθῆναι Θεὸς κατεδέξατο· ἢ πότε Θεὸς γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος· ἢ πότε Θεὸς ἀσπόρως συνείληπται· ἢ πότε κυοφορίας Θεὸς ἠνέσχετο· ἢ πότε Θεὸς σαρκικὴν ἐκ γυναικὸς ἐπεδείξατο γέννησιν· ἢ πότε Θεὸς γυναικείων μαζῶν ἀπεθήλασεν· ἢ πότε Θεὸς κλαυθμυρισμὸν ἀπηνέγκατο· ἢ πότε Θεὸς ἡλικίας αὔξησιν εἴληφεν· ἢ πότε Θεὸς σὺν τοῖς ἀνθρώποις ἡμῖν Cod. ἡμῖν. πεπολίτευται· ἢ πότε Θεὸς ὑπὲρ ἀνθρώπων ἀπέθανεν· ἢ πότε Θεὸς σταυροῦ προσήλωσιν ἔσχηκεν· ἢ πότε Θεὸς λόγχῃ τὴν πλευρὰν ἐκκεκέντηται· ἢ πότε Θεὸς τριήμερον ταφὴν ὑπελήλυθεν· ἢ πότεΘεὸς πάντα ταῦτα πάσχων τεθέαται, ἵνα παθῶν ἁπάντων τὸν ἄνθρωπον 3272ῥύσηται, καὶ θανάτου μὲν καὶ φθορᾶς ὀλοθρεύσῃ τὴν ἔφοδον, ἄφθαρτον Excidisse hinc videtur particula δέ. καὶ ἀθάνατον ἀναστήσῃ τὴν οἰκείαν εἰκόνα, τὸν ἄνθρωπον. Εἰ τοίνυν μηδέποτε ταῦτα, ὦ Παρθένε, γεγένηται, εἰς τὸν παρόντα δ καιρὸν ἐτετήρητο, καὶ πληροῦσθαι διὰ σοῦ προτεθέσπιστο· πῶς ἂν ἄλλη παρθένος σου προτέρα γεγέννηκεν, ὅτι τοῦτο παρ᾿ ἐμοῦ μαθεῖν ἐπεζήτησας πρὸς πίστωσιν καὶ παράστασιν τῆς σῆς παρθενικῆς ἀποτέξεως; Οὐ τέτοκέποτε παρθένος, ὦ Παρθένε, οὐ τέτοκεν· ἀλλ᾿ οὔτε μετὰ σὲ παρθένος ἑτέρα, ὦ Παρθένε, πώποτε τέξεται· ἀλλ᾿ οὔτε Θεομήτωρ, ὦ Παρθένε, παρβενος γεγένηται· οὔτε μετὰ σὲ παρθένος τις ἄλλη, ὦ Παρθένε, τὴν μόνην Θεομήτορα, γίνεται Aut excidit, aut certe subintelligitur hic repetita vox Θεομήτωρ.. Σὺ γὰρ καὶ μόνη γυναικῶν παρθένος γεννήσειας, καὶ σύ γε μόνη Θεομήτωρ ἐν γυναιξὶ χρηματίσειας. «Ἅπαξ γὰρ ἐπὶ συντελείᾳ τῶν αἰώνων» ὁ μονογενὴς Υἱὸς τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ Πατρὸς ἐνανθρωπήσει, καὶ τὴν ἐκ σοῦ καὶ μονῆς θαυμαστὴν ἐπιδείξεται

ΜΑʹ. Καὶ ὅτι τοῦτο Θεοῦ τὸ ἐπάγγελμα διὰ σοῦ καὶ μόνης τὴν πλήρωσιν ἔμελλε δέξασθαι, προφητικοῖς σε πιστώσομαι ῥήμασιν. Ἡσαΐας γοῦν ὁ θαυμάσιος τὴν ἐκ σοῦ τοῦ Λόγου καὶ Θεοῦ προορώμενος σάρκωσιν, σύλληψίν τε θείαν καὶ ἄσπορον, καὶ κυοφορίαν ἀνώδυνον, καὶ γέννησιν ἄφραστον, καὶ γαλούχησιν ἄλεκτον, ὧδε φανερῶς προανακέκραγεν· «Ἰδοὺ ἡ παρθένος ἐν γαστρὶ ἕξει, καὶ τέξεται υἱὸν, καὶ καλέσουσι τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ Ἐμμανουήλ.» Ὁρᾷς, Παρθένε ἁγνὴ καὶ ἀμόλυντε, ὡς παρθένον ἀναντιῤῥήτως προηγόρευσε τὴν τὸν Ἐμμανουὴλ τέξεσθαι μέλλουσαν; ἥτις σὺ καὶ μόνη καθέστηκας, καὶ οὐχ ἑτέρα τις πρὸ σοῦ ἢ μετὰ σὲ θεαθήσεται. Οὐ γὰρ, Ἰδοὺ αἱ παρθένοι, φησὶν ὁ θεόπνευστος, ἐν γαστρὶ ἕξουσι, καὶ τέξονται υἱοὺς, καὶ καλέσουσι τὰ ὀνόματα αὐτῶν Ἐμμανουὴλ, ἵνα καὶ ἄλλην παρθένον παραστήσω σοι τίκτουσαν. Εἷς γὰρ ὁ Ἐμμανουὴλ, ὃν σὺ σαρκικῶς, ὦ Παρθένε, γεννήσειας, καὶ οὐκ ἄλλος ὁ Ἐμμανουὴλ ἢ πρότερον γέγονεν, ἢ μετὰ ταῦτα γενήσεται, ἵνα καὶ ἄλλη παρθένος τόκον τέκοι παράδοξον· καὶ μία σὺ εἶ, ὦ Παρθένε, ἡ τούτου θεία γεννήτρια Cod. γεννήτρεια., καὶ οὐκ ἄλλη τις πρὸ σοῦ Θεομήτωρ πεφήμισται, ἢ μετὰ σὲ προσγενήσεται.

ΜΒʹ. Οὐκοῦν κάλλιστά σοι καὶ πρεπωδέστατα κέκραγα· «Χαῖρε, κεχαριτωμένη, ὁ Κύριος μετὰ σοῦ.» Τὸ γὰρ Ἐμμανουὴλ Ἑβραϊκῇ διαλέκτῳ λεγόμενον, εἰς Ἑλλάδα φωνὴν ἀμειβόμενον, «Μεθ᾿ ἡμῶν ὁ Θεὸς,» ἐκλαμβάνεται.

Or when was God seen suffering all these things, that he might rescue man from all passions, 3272and destroy the assault of death and corruption, and raise up incorruptible and immortal his own image, man? If then these things, O Virgin, have never come to pass, but had been kept for the present time, and had been foretold to be fulfilled through thee, how could another virgin before thee have given birth, that thou shouldst seek to learn this from me for assurance and proof of thy virginal childbearing? Never has a virgin borne, O Virgin, never has she borne; nor will another virgin after thee, O Virgin, ever bear; nor, O Virgin, has a virgin become Mother of God; nor after thee, the only Mother of God, O Virgin, does any other virgin become so. For thou alone of women wouldst give birth as a virgin, and thou alone among women wouldst be styled Mother of God. «For once, at the consummation of the ages,» the only-begotten Son of God and the Father will become man, and will show forth the wonderful [?] from thee and thee alone.

41. And that this promise of God was to receive its fulfilment through thee and thee alone, I shall assure thee by prophetic words. Isaiah, then, the wonderful, foreseeing the incarnation of the Word and God from thee, and a conception divine and seedless, and a child-bearing painless, and a birth unutterable, and a suckling unspeakable, thus clearly cried out beforehand: «Behold, the virgin shall have in the womb, and shall bear a son, and they shall call his name Emmanuel.» Seest thou, Virgin pure and undefiled, how he incontestably proclaimed a virgin her who was to bear Emmanuel? Who thou alone art, and no other before thee or after thee will be beheld. For the God-inspired one does not say, "Behold, the virgins shall have in the womb, and shall bear sons, and they shall call their names Emmanuel," so that I might present to thee another virgin also giving birth. For one is Emmanuel, whom thou, O Virgin, shalt beget according to the flesh, and no other Emmanuel either came to be before or will come to be after these things, so that another virgin also might bear a paradoxical childbirth; and one art thou, O Virgin, his divine begetter, and no other before thee has been reported Mother of God, or will be added after thee.

42. Therefore most finely and most fittingly I have cried to thee: «Hail, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee.» For Emmanuel, spoken in the Hebrew tongue, being changed into the Greek speech, is taken as «God with us.»

Πότε δὲ Θεὸς μετὰ ἀνθρώπων γεγένηται, καὶ τοῖς ἀνθρώποις ὑμῖν συνηρίθμηται Cod. συναρίθμηται. 3273εἰ μὴ νῦν ἐν ἐσχάτοις καιροῖς τοῦ αἰῶνος, ὅτε καὶ σπέρματος Ἀβραὰμ ἐπελάβετο,» καὶ σάρκα τὴν ἐκ σοῦ προσανείληφε; Τὸ γὰρ Ἐμμανουὴλ ἐξηγούμενον, διττῆς ἑρμηνείας καθέστηκε· φύσεως ἀνθρωπίνης καὶ θείας, ἐξ ὧν καὶ συντέθειται, ἐν αἷς καὶ γνωρίζεται. Τέλειος γὰρ ὢν Θεὸς προαιώνιος, τέλειος ἐν σοὶ γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος, τὴν ἀνθρωπείαν φύσιν ἐκ σοῦ προσλαβόμενος, καὶ ταύτην ἑαυτῷ συνθεὶς καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν φυλάξας Cod. φυλλάξας. ἀναλλοίωτον, τὴν φυσικὴν ἑκατέρας ποιότητα φύσεως, καὶ τὰ τούτων φυσικὰ καὶ οὐσιώδη γνωρίσματα. Ὅ τε γὰρ Θεὸς Λόγος, Θεὸς Λόγος μεμένηκε, καὶ εἰ φύσιν σαρκὸς φυσικῶς ἠμφιάσατο· ἤ τε σὰρξ ἐμψυχωμένη λογικῶς, ἣν ἐκ τῶν σῶν ἀχράντων αἱμάτων προσείληφε, καὶ πνεύματι ζωῆς λογικῆς ἐψύχωσε, σὰρξ λογικῶς ἐμψυχωμένη μεμένηκε, καὶ εἰ μόνου Θεοῦ Λόγου σὰρξ ἐγνωρίζετο, ἡνωμένη αὐτῷ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν, καὶ εἰς ἑνὸς προσώπου καὶ μιᾶς ὑποστάσεως συνδρομὴν οὐ τεμνομένην συνθέουσα, οὐδὲ μὴν τὰ ἑνούμενα τρέπουσαν, καὶ εἰς οὐσίας ταυτότητα φέρουσαν. Εἰ καὶ ἑκατέρων οὖν ὁ Χριστὸς διεφύλαξε τῶν φύσεων καὶ οὐσιῶν τὸ διάφορον· εἷς Υἱὸς καὶ Χριστὸς ἐκ σοῦ τῷ κόσμῳ φανήσεται, οὐ δεικνὺς υἱῶν καὶ χριστῶν τὸ διάφορον. Ὁ γὰρ ὢν Θεὸς ἀληθὴς, ἄνθρωπος ἀληθὴς καὶ τεχθήσεται, ψεῦδος οὐδὲν ἐνδεικνύμενος, ὧν ἂν καλοῖτο καὶ λέγοιτο. Διὰ τοῦτο Ἐμμανουὴλ προσκληθήσεται, ὡς ἄμφω πεφυκὼς κατὰ ἀλήθειαν, καὶ Θεὸς ὁ αὐτὸς ἀπερίγραφος, καὶ ἄνθρωπος ὁ αὐτὸς κατὰ ἀλήθειαν· τὸ μὲν κατὰ τὴν ἐκ Πατρὸς ἀΐδιον γέννησιν, τὸ δὲ κατὰ τὴν ἐκ σοῦ τῆς Μητρὸς χρονικὴν καὶ ἐνσώματον κύησιν. Οὕτω γὰρ σώζειν τὴν ἀνθρωπείαν φύσιν ηὐδόκησεν· ὅτιπερ εἰς τοσαύτην ἑαυτὸν καθῆκεν ὁ ἀκένωτος κένωσιν, ἵνα τὴν κενωθεῖσαν εἰς γῆν διαφθορᾶς ἀνθρωπότητα, πάλιν ἐκ γῆς ἀναστήσῃ, καὶ οὐρανίων κλήρων ποιήσῃ μέτοχον, ἀντὶ τῆς ἐν παραδείσῳ τὸ πρὶν ἀπολαύσεως

ΜΓʹ. Καὶ πῶς τοῦτο, ὦ θεοδόχε Παρθένε, γενήσεται, ἐρῶ καὶ φράζω τοῖς ῥήμασιν, ἐπειδὴ καὶ τοῦτο πρὸς σὲ λέξων ἐλήλυθα· «Πνεῦμα ἅγιον ἐπελεύσεται ἐπὶ σὲ, καὶ δύναμις Ὑψίστου ἐπισκιάσεισοι· διὸ καὶ τὸ γεννώμενον ἅγιον, κληθήσεται Υἱὸς Θεοῦ.»

Ἔχεις σαφῆ τὴν ἀπόδειξιν, ἔχεις ἀληθῆ τὴν ἀγόρευσιν. Πνεῦμα ἅγιον ἐπὶ σὲ, τὴν ἀμόλυντον, κάτεισι, καθαρωτέραν σε ποιησόμενον, καὶ καρπογόνον σοι παρεξόμενον δύναμιν. «Ἐπισκιάσει δέ σοι ἡ Θεοῦ» παντοδύναμος «δύναμις,» τοῦτ᾿ ἔστιν, ὁ τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγος καὶ Υἱὸς ὁμοούσιος.

But when has God come to be with men, and been numbered with you men, 3273except now in the last times of the age, when he also «took hold of the seed of Abraham,» and took to himself the flesh from thee?

For Emmanuel, interpreted, is of twofold meaning: of human and of divine nature, from which he is also composed, in which he is also made known. For being perfect God before the ages, he became perfect man in thee, taking to himself the human nature from thee, and having compounded it with himself according to hypostasis, keeping unalterable the natural quality of each nature, and their natural and essential marks. For both God the Word has remained God the Word, even if he naturally clothed himself in the nature of flesh; and the flesh rationally ensouled, which he took to himself from thy stainless blood, and ensouled with the spirit of rational life, has remained flesh rationally ensouled, even if it was made known as the flesh of God the Word alone, united to him according to hypostasis, and running together into the uncut concourse of one person and one hypostasis, nor indeed changing the things united, and bearing them into identity of essence.

Even if, then, Christ preserved the difference of both the natures and essences, one Son and Christ will appear from thee to the world, not showing a difference of sons and christs. For he who is true God will also be born true man, showing no falsehood in whatever he may be called and said. For this reason he will be called Emmanuel, as being by nature both in truth: both God, the same, uncircumscribed, and man, the same, in truth; the one according to the eternal birth from the Father, the other according to the temporal and embodied gestation from thee, the Mother. For so he was well pleased to save human nature: in that he who cannot be emptied let himself down into so great an emptying, that the manhood emptied into the earth of corruption he might raise again from the earth, and make it partaker of heavenly inheritances, in place of the enjoyment in paradise before.

43. And how this shall come about, O God-receiving Virgin, I will tell and declare in words, since I have also come to say this to thee: «The Holy Spirit shall come upon thee, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow thee; wherefore also that which is born holy shall be called Son of God.»

Thou hast a clear demonstration, thou hast a true declaration. The Holy Spirit comes down upon thee, the undefiled, to make thee purer, and to furnish thee with fruit-bearing power. «And» the all-powerful «power of God shall overshadow thee,» that is, the Word and consubstantial Son of God.

Οὗτος γὰρ αὐτοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ Πατρὸς καὶ χείρ ἐστι καὶ βραχίων καὶ δύναμις, 3276καὶ δι᾿ αὐτοῦ ὁ Πατὴρ, ὡς δι᾿ Υἱοῦ, τὰ πάντα πεποίηκε, καὶ«ἐν αὐτῷ τὰ πάντα συνέστηκε,» τὴν ἐκ σοῦ ποιησόμενος ἀνερμήνευτον σάρκωσιν, καὶ τὴν ἐκ σοῦ ἐνδειξόμενος ἀγεώργητον σύλληψιν, καὶ τὴν ἐκ σοῦ ἐργασόμενος ἀπερινόητον γέννησιν.

ΜΔʹ. «Διὸ καὶ τὸ γεννώμενον ἅγιον, κληθήσεται Υἱὸς Θεοῦ.» Εἰ τοίνυν Θεοῦ Υἱὸς, ὁ ἐκ σοῦ τεχθησόμενος τῆς Θεοτόκου Παρθένου, κληθήσεται, πῶς οὐ παρθένος ἡ τοῦτον γεννῶσα φανήσεται; Δεῖγμα γὰρ τοῦτο σαφὲς καὶ ἀκράδαντον γνώρισμα ἔσται τῆς αὐτοῦ τῶν πάντων κρατούσης θεότητος, τὸ παρθένον σε τυγχάνουσαν ἄσπιλον τεκεῖν τὴν ἀνέκφραστον γέννησιν, καὶ παρθένον μεῖναι μετὰ τὴν ἄφθορον λόχευσιν. Εἰ γὰρ Θεὸς, ὁ ἐκ σοῦ τῆς παρθένου τικτόμενος, ὥσπερ οὖν καὶ Θεὸς ἔστι καὶ κηρύττεται, τί οὐκ ἂν βουληθεὶς διαπράξαιτο; Θεὸς γάρ ἐστι, κἂν εἰ καὶ ὡς ἄνθρωπος τίκτηται· καὶ ἄνθρωπός ἐστι, κἂν εἰ καὶ ὡς Θεὸς παρθένον σε φυλάττῃ τὴν τίκτουσαν. Διὰ τοῦτο γὰρ καὶ αὐτὸς ἐγὼ Γαβριὴλ πρὸς σὲ τὴν Παρθένον ἀπέσταλμαι, ἵνα μου καὶ αὐτῷ δηλῶ τῷ ὀνόματι, ὡς Θεὸς ὑπάρχει καὶ ἄνθρωπος ὁ ἐκ σοῦ τῆς ἀχράντου Παρθένου τικτόμενος. Καὶ γὰρ καὶ τὸ Γαβριὴλ ὄνομα εἰς ἑρμηνείαν ἀγόμενον, Θεὸν ἀγορεύει καὶ ἄνθρωπον. Ἐπεὶ πολλοὶ κατ᾿ οὐρανὸν Nic. κατ᾿ οὐρανὸν πολλοί. ἐτύγχανον ἄγγελοι, ἀλλ᾿ οὐδένα τούτων στεῖλαι βε-

βούλευται· ἀλλ᾿ ἐμὲ, τὸν τούτων ἕναφαινόμενον, Γαβριὴλ δὲ ὀνομαζόμενον, ἔπεμψεν, ἵνα καὶ λόγῳ τῷ ἐμῷ καὶ ὀνόματι δηλώσω σοι σαφῶς τὸν ἐκ σοῦ τεχθησόμενον, ὡς Θεός ἐστι καὶ ἄνθρωπος, καὶ «πάντα φέρει τῷ τῆς οἰκείας δυνάμεως ῥήματι·» καὶ, ὡς ἅγιος εἴη, καὶ τῶν ἀπάντων ἀνθρώπων ἁγιάσοι τὴν γέννησιν, καὶ ὡς Υἱὸς Ὑψίστου κληθήσεται, καὶ υἱοὺς Ὑψίστου ποιήσειε χάριτι τοὺς εἰς αὐτὸν πιστεύοντας. Λοιπὸν τοῖς ἐμοῖς, ὦ Παρθένε, πίστευε ῥήμασι· πάντως γὰρ οὗτοι Ita cod. et subintellig. λόγοι. πραγματικῶς πληρωθήσονται. Ἀληθεύω γάρ σοι φθεγγόμενος, ὡς ἀληθείας κήρυξ καὶ ἄγγελος.

For he is, of God himself and the Father, both hand and arm and 3276power, and through him the Father, as through a Son, has made all things, and «in him all things consist» — he who will make the uninterpretable incarnation from thee, and show forth the untilled conception from thee, and work the inconceivable birth from thee.

44. «Wherefore also that which is born holy shall be called Son of God.» If then he who will be born of thee, the Theotokos Virgin, shall be called Son of God, how will she who bears him not appear a virgin? For this will be a clear proof and an unshaken mark of his Godhead that rules all things: that thou, being a spotless virgin, bearest the inexpressible birth, and remainest a virgin after the incorrupt delivery. For if he who is born of thee the virgin is God, as indeed he both is and is proclaimed God, what would he not accomplish if he willed? For he is God, even if he is also born as man; and he is man, even if also as God he keeps thee a virgin who bearest.

For this reason I myself, Gabriel, have been sent to thee the Virgin, that by my very name I might show that he who is born of thee, the stainless Virgin, is God and man. For indeed the name Gabriel, brought to interpretation, declares God and man. Since many angels were in heaven, but he did not will to send any one of these; but me, who appear one of them, and am named Gabriel, he sent, that by my word and name I might clearly show thee the one to be born of thee, that he is God and man, and «bears all things by the word of his own power;» and that he is holy, and would sanctify the birth of all men, and that he shall be called Son of the Most High, and would make sons of the Most High by grace those who believe in him. For the rest, O Virgin, believe my words; for they will surely be fulfilled in deed. For I speak truth in what I utter to thee, as herald and angel of truth.

Καὶ πρὸς σὴν πληροφορίαν καὶ πίστωσιν μείζονα τῆς τῶν ἐμῶν ῥημάτων πληρώσεως, καὶ σημεῖον ἀναντίῤῥητον δίδωμι, ὅπως μένοις περὶ τὴν πίστιν ἀσάλευτος· «Ἰδοὺ γὰρ Ἐλισάβετ, ἡ συγγενής σου, καὶ αὐτὴ συνειληφυῖα υἱὸν ἐν γήρει αὐτῆς· καὶ οὗτος μὴν ἕκτος ἐστὶν αὐτῇ τῇ καλουμένῃ στείρῃ· ὅτι οὐκ 3277ἀδυνατήσει παρὰ τῷ Θεῷ πᾶν ῥῆμα.»Καὶ τοῦτο σοι δεῖγμα γενήσεται κράτιστον, ὡς πάντα μου τὰ ῥήματα, πρὸς σὲ, τὴν πᾶσι σεπτὴν, εὐαγγέλια, τέλος ἀληθὲς καὶ σωτήριον δέξεται πρὸς ἀνθρώπων ἁπάντων ζωὴν καὶ ἐκλύτρωσιν. Τῆς γὰρ αὐτῆς παρὰ Θεῷ τυγχάνει δυνάμεως, καὶ τὸ, στείραν τεκεῖν τὴν οὐ τίκτουσαν, καὶ τὸ, παρθένον τεκεῖν τὴν ἀνύμφευτον. Σὺ οὖν χαῖρε, ὦ κεχαριτωμένη παρθένε καὶ ἔνθεε, ἐφ᾿ οἷς σοι Θεὸς ὁ τῶν ὅλων Δεσπότης κεχάρισται, καὶ ἐφ᾿ οἷς Θεοῦ τὸν Υἱὸν, τὸν πάντων χαρὰν γενησόμενον τῶν ἀνθρώπων τῷ γένει, γεννήσειας. ΜΕʹ Πρὸς ὃν ἡ Θεομήτωρ Παρθένος πιστεύσασα, καὶ ὡς ἔσται πάντως τελείωσις τοῖς λελαλημένοις αὐτῇ παρὰ Κυρίου, θαρσήσασα, πρᾴως ὁμοῦ καὶ ταπεινῶς, οὐ μὴν ἀλλὰ καὶ παρθενικῶς ἀποκρίνεται· τὸν «πρᾶον γὰρ καὶ ταπεινὸν τῇ καρδίᾳ» Χριστὸν γεννᾷν ἡ πάναγνος ἔμελλε, καὶ πλουτεῖν αὐτὴν ἔδει τὴν αὐτοῦ ταπεινότητα καὶ πραότητα· «Ἰδοὺ ἡ δούλη Κυρίου· γένοιτό μοι κατὰ τὸ ῥῆμά σου.»

Μϛʹ. Γαβριὴλ μὲν οὖν ὁ ἀρχάγγελος ταῦτα πρὸς τῆς Θεοτόκου Παρθένου Cod. πρὸς τὴν Θεοτόκον Παρθένον. Idem et in Cæsar, codicis fragmento legitur. πυθόμενος, εἰς οὐρανὸν ἀνελήλυθεν, ὅθεν πρὸς αὐτὴν καὶ ἀπέσταλτο Cod. Cæs, ἀπέσταλται.. Ἡ δὲ Θεὸν ἐκυοφόρει τὸν στείλαντα, τὸν ἄνω μὲν σὺν Πατρὶ δοξαζόμενον, κάτω δὲ καὶ ἐξ αὐτῆς, σαρκικῆς κυοφορίας ἀνεχόμενον. Ἅμα γὰρ ὁ Λόγος καὶ Θεὸς πρὸς αὐτὴν παραγέγονε, καὶ διὰ τῆς τοῦ ἀγγέλου φωνῆς, εἰς τὴν θείαν αὐτῆς νηδὺν εἰσελήλυθε καὶ σεσάρκωτο, καὶ ἄνευ τροπῆς σεσωμάτωτο, σάρκα προσλαβὼν ἐξ αὐτῆς τὴν ἀνήροτον, καὶ ταύτην ἑνώσας ἑαυτῷ κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν, λογικῶς μὲν ἐμψυχωμένην, οὐ προϋποστᾶσαν δὲ τῆς Cod. τήν. πρὸς αὐτὸν ἀνεκφράστου συνθέσεως. Ἅμα γὰρ σὰρξ, ἅμα Θεοῦ Λόγου σάρξ· ἅμα σὰρξ ἔμψυχος λογική Nic. pag. 221 addit, ἅμα Θεοῦ Λόγου σάρξ· sed forte e præcedentibus, quæ ipse non affert, huc ea transtulit.· ἐν αὐτῷ Nic. ἑαυτῷ. γὰρ τὴν ὑπόστασιν Nic. intra parenthesim addit, ἤτοι τὸ εἶναι, sed plane inscite; caro enim in Verbo quidem subsistebat, sed in se erat, atque esse sui proprium habebat : nec minus inscite, uti patet, illud αὐτῷ immutavit in ἑαυτῷ., καὶ οὐ πρὸ αὐτοῦ τὴν σύστασιν ἔσχηκεν. Οὔτε τινὸς ἑτέρου σὰρξ τῶν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς ἀνθρώπων γέγονε πώποτε, καὶ τότε τῷ Θεῷ Λόγῳ συνέδραμε Nic. sequens incisum omittit., καὶ τὴν πρὸς αὐτὸν ἐποιήσατο σύνθεσιν· ἀλλ᾿ ἐν τῷ Nic. ἐν αὐτῷ τῷ., τὸ εἶναι σὰρξ ἔμψυχος λογικὴ, καὶ Θεοῦ Λόγου σὰρξ ἔμψυχος λογικὴ ἐκ παρθενικῶν καὶ ἀχράντων αἱμάτων ληφθεῖσα τῆς ἀχράντου Παρθένου κεκλήρωκεν.

And for thy full assurance and a greater confirmation of the fulfilment of my words, I give also an incontestable sign, so that thou mayest remain unshaken in the faith: «For behold, Elisabeth thy kinswoman, she too has conceived a son in her old age; and this is the sixth month with her who is called barren; for 3277no word shall be impossible with God.» And this will be to thee a most mighty proof that all my words, good tidings to thee, august to all, will receive a true and saving end, for the life and redemption of all men. For it belongs to the same power with God both that a barren woman should bear, she who does not bear, and that a virgin should bear, she who is unwedded. Hail thou, then, O thou that art full of grace, virgin and God-filled, for the things in which God, the Master of all, has shown thee grace, and in which thou wouldst beget the Son of God, who shall become joy to all the race of men.

45. To whom the Virgin Mother of God, believing, and taking courage that there would surely be a fulfilment of the things spoken to her from the Lord, answers meekly and humbly together, nay more, virginally too; for the all-pure was to bear the Christ «meek and lowly in heart,» and she had to be rich in his lowliness and meekness: «Behold the handmaid of the Lord; be it to me according to thy word.»

46. Gabriel the archangel, then, having learned these things from the Theotokos Virgin, went up into heaven, whence he had also been sent to her. And she bore in her womb God who sent him, who is glorified above with the Father, and below, and from her, endures fleshly child-bearing. For at once the Word and God came to her, and through the voice of the angel entered her divine womb and was incarnate, and without change was embodied, taking to himself from her the unploughed flesh, and uniting it to himself in truth, rationally ensouled indeed, but not subsisting before its inexpressible composition with him. For at once flesh, at once flesh of God the Word; at once flesh ensouled, rational; for it had its hypostasis in him, and not its constitution before him.

Nor was it ever the flesh of any other of the men like us, and then ran together with God the Word, and made its composition with him; but in its being flesh ensouled and rational, it was also allotted to be flesh of God the Word, ensouled and rational, taken from the virginal and stainless blood of the stainless Virgin.

Οὐδεμία γὰρ τοῖς διαιρεῖν τὸν ἕνα Υἱὸν καὶ Χριστὸν εἰς δύο υἱοὺς καὶ Χριστοὺς ἀσεβέστατα θέλουσιν ἔμΑφασις 3280ἢ πρόφασις ἔνεστιν, ὡς ὑπέστη ποτὲ καθ᾿ ἐατὴν, καὶ ἴδιον ἔσχηκε πρόσωπον, καὶ εἶθ᾿ οὕτως τῷ Λόγῳ καὶ Θεῷ πρὸς ἕνωσιν φυσικὴν συνελήλυθε. Τὰ γὰρ ἀνὰ μέρος ὄντα, καὶ ἀπ᾿ ἀλλήλων ἐν χωρισμῷ θεωρούμενα, οὐδέποτε φυσικὴν, ἢ τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν δέχεται.

For those who most impiously wish to divide the one Son and Christ into two sons and Christs have no 3280indication or pretext that it ever subsisted by itself, and had its own person, and then thus came together with the Word and God into natural union. For things that are apart, and are beheld in separation from one another, never receive natural union, or that according to hypostasis.

Ὅπου δὲ ἕνωσις φυσικὴ μὴ προέρχεται, μηδ᾿ ἡ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν γίνεται σύνθεσις, οὐδὲ μία καὶ ἡ αὐτὴ ὑπόστασις δείκνυται, οὔτε ἓν καὶ τὸ αὐτὸ καταγγέλλεται πρόσωπον, ἀδιαίρετον ὁμοῦ καὶ ἀμέριστον, καὶ πᾶσαν τομὴν διωθούμενον.

3281ΜΖʹ. Ὥσπερ δὲ τοὺς τοιούτους παράφρονας ἡ ἀδιαίρετος ἐκκλίνει τοῦ Λόγου καὶ ἀμέριστος σάρκωσις, καὶ πάντη τῶν τῆς εὐσεβείας τροφίμων ἐξίστησι (Νεστόριοι … συνόμιλοι), οὗτοι καὶ Παῦλοί εἰσι, καὶ Θεόδωροι, καὶ οἳ τούτων πρὸς θεομάχον μανίαν συνόμιλλοι Ita cod. pro συνόμιλοι, vel forte pro συνάμιλλοι, quanquam usitatum reperiamus quidem ἀμιλλότερος, non vero ἄμιλλος., οὕτω καὶ τοὺς συγχέειν τὰς φύσεις θέλοντας, καὶ φύσιν μίαν ἐξ ἀμφοτέρων ἐργάσασθαι σπεύδοντας φύσεων, συλλόγων ἔξω τῶν εὐσεβῶν ἀποπέμπεται. Οὔτε γὰρ, κατὰ τὴν ἐκείνων μανίαν, ὁ Λόγος σαρκούμενος, τρέψας ἑαυτοῦ πρὸς σάρκα τὴν φύσιν σεσάρκωται, ἀλλ᾿ οὔτε τὴν σάρκα τὴν ἔννουν καὶ ἔμψυχον, ἣν ἐκ Παρθένου ταυτησὶ τῆς Θεοτόκου προσείληφε, πρὸς φύσιν ἑαυτοῦ τὴν θείαν· ἠλλοίωσε, καὶ θεϊκὴν πρὸς οὐσίαν μετέβαλεν· ἀλλ᾿ ἔμεινεν ἄτρεπτος, καὶ μεταβολῆς παντοίας ἀνώτερος, καὶ συγχύσεως πάσης ἐλεύθερος, καὶ σάρκα σαρκωθεὶς τὴν ἡμῖν ὁμοούσιον. καὶ γενόμενος δι᾿ ἡμᾶς κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν ἄνθρωπος. Μεμένηκε δὲ καὶ ἡ σὰρξ ἀναλλοίωτος, καὶ πάσης ἔξω τροπῆς καὶ συγχύσεως· καὶ τῷ λόγῳ συμβᾶσα πρὸς σύστασιν, καὶ ἑνωθεῖσα καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν αὐτῷ τὴν ἀμέριστον, ἔχουσα μὲν τὸ πρὸς ἡμᾶς ὁμοούσιον ἄτρεπτον, καὶ σώζουσα, ὅσον εἰς φύσιν καὶ οὐσίαν καὶ ὕπαρξιν, ἀπαράλλακτον πρὸς ἡμᾶς τὴν ὁμοίωσιν, οὐδένα δὲ τοῦ Λόγου μερισμὸν ὑπομένουσα, ἢ τομὴν καὶ διαίρεσιν πάσχουσα· ἀλλ᾿ αὐτοῦ τοῦ Λόγου ἰδία καὶ ἰδικὴ χρηματίζουσα, καὶ πρὸς ὑπόστασιν αὐτῷ μίαν συντρέχουσα, διὰ τὴν πρὸς αὐτὸν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν, ἐπὶ τῶν ἰδίων ὅρων τῆς φύσεως ἑστῶσα καὶ μένουσα δείκνυται· διαφορὰν μὲν φυσικὴν πρὸς αὐτὸν ὑποφαίνουσα, διαφορὰν δὲ προσωπικὴν πρὸς αὐτὸν οὐ γινώσκουσα, ἀλλ᾿ εἰς μίαν μὲν σὺν αὐτῷ τελοῦσα καὶ μόνην ὑπόστασιν, καὶ ταύτην ἑνιαίαν καὶ σύνθετον (οὐ γὰρ ἁπλῆν καὶ ἀσύνθετον), οὐκ εἰς μίαν δὲ φύσιν συνθέουσα σύνθετον· ἀρχὴ γὰρ τοῦτο τυγχάνει συγχύσεως, καὶ κρηπὶς ὑπάρχει τρεπτικῆς ἀλλοιώσεως.

And where natural union does not go before, nor composition according to hypostasis come about, neither is one and the same hypostasis shown, nor one and the same person announced, undivided together and impartible, and thrusting away every cutting.

47. 3281And just as the undivided and impartible incarnation of the Word turns away such madmen, and wholly removes them from the nurslings of piety (these are the Nestoriuses and Pauls and Theodores, and those who are their associates in God-fighting madness), so also those who wish to confuse the natures, and hasten to make one nature out of both natures, it sends away outside the assemblies of the pious. For neither, according to their madness, did the Word, in being incarnate, become incarnate by changing his nature into flesh; nor did he alter the flesh endowed with mind and soul, which he took to himself from this Virgin, the Theotokos, into his own divine nature, and transform it into a divine essence; but he remained unchangeable, and above every transformation, and free of all confusion, and was incarnate in flesh consubstantial with us, and became for our sakes man in truth. And the flesh too has remained unalterable, and outside all change and confusion; and having come together with the Word into constitution, and having been united to him according to the impartible hypostasis, having indeed its consubstantiality with us unchangeable, and preserving, so far as nature and essence and existence go, its likeness to us without variation, but undergoing no partition from the Word, or suffering cutting and division; but being styled the Word's own and peculiar flesh, and running together with him into one hypostasis on account of the union with him according to hypostasis, it is shown standing and remaining within the proper bounds of its nature; displaying a natural difference towards him, but knowing no personal difference towards him; but completing with him one and only hypostasis, and this unitary and composite (for not simple and incomposite), but not running together into one composite nature; for this is the beginning of confusion, and is the foundation of changeful alteration.

Ὥσπερ οὖν τοὺς διαιροῦντας καὶ τέμνοντας ὁ Λόγος σαρκωθεὶς ἀποσείεται, οὕτω καὶ τοὺς ἄμφω τὰς φύσεις συγχέοντας, καὶ κρᾶσιν αὐτῶν καὶ ἀλλοίωσιν φάσκοντας, περιβόλων ἱερῶν ἀποβάλλεται Cod. Cæs. ἀποφαίνεται.· Σευῆροι οὗτοι Codex Cæsareus duplici mendo sic binas voces in unam contrahit : σευχροιοῦτοι. καὶ Εὐτυχεῖς, καὶ Διόσκοροι, Ἀπολινάριοί 3284τε καὶ Πολέμωνες, καὶ πρό γε τούτων Σίμωνες, Οὐαλεντῖνοι, Μαρκίωνες, Βασιλεῖδαι, Μανέντες τε καὶ Μαίνανδροι, καὶ ἡ νῦν κακῶς ἀνασκάλλουσα, πᾶσα τῶν Ἀκεφάλων αἵρεσις πολυκέφαλος· ἡ ἕνα μὲν πόλεμον καὶ κοινὸν πρὸς τὴν εὐσέβειαν ἔχουσα, μυρίους δὲ πρὸς ἑαυτὴν καὶ παυομένους Cod. Cæs. πανομοίους. μηδέποτε φέρουσα. Ταῦτα οὖν τὰ ὄντως ἱερὰ καὶ πανίερα, καὶ ἀκοῶν ἐνθέων ἐπάξια τῶν εὐσεβούντων ἡμῶν εὐαγγέλια, ἡμᾶς μὲν ἡδέως προσίεται, ἐκείνους δὲ τοὺς μιαροὺς καὶ παράφρονας ἔξω διώκει ταυτησὶ τῆς εὐαγγελίου αὐτῶν πανηγύρεως, ὡς ἀσεβῶς αὐτὰ νοοῦντας ἐκείνους καὶ λέγοντας.

ΜΗʹ. Ἐγὼ δὲ ὁ ἐλάχιστος, ὁ τούτων προελθὼν κήρυξ τήμερον, πρὸς μόνους ὑμᾶς, τοὺς εὐσεβείας παῖδας καὶ πίστεως, καὶ δεχομένους εὐσεβῶς τὰ λεγόμενα, μεγαλοφώνως βοῶ, καὶ διαῤῥήδην κηρύττω καὶ φθέγγομαι· «Εὐαγγέλια, εὐαγγέλια, εὐαγγέλια.» Εὐαγγέλια τοῦ Σωτῆρος ἡμῶν, εὐαγγέλια τοῦ σαρκωθέντος Θεοῦ δι᾿ ἡμᾶς· εὐαγγέλια τῆς Θεοῦ παρουσίας πρὸς ἡμᾶς· εὐαγγέλια ἐνανθρωπήσεως Θεοῦ τῆς ἐξ ἡμῶν· εὐαγγέλια ἀσπόρου συλλήψεως Θεοῦ· εὐαγγέλια κυοφορίας Θεοῦ παρθενικῆς· εὐαγγέλια ἀτρέπτου συνδρομῆς Θεοῦ πρὸς σάρκα· εὐαγγέλια, ἐφ᾿ οἷς ἡμεῖς οἱ ἀπόβλητοι ὑψούμεθα καὶ θεούμεθα καὶ θεοὶ κατὰ χάριν γινόμεθα.

So then, just as the Word incarnate shakes off those who divide and cut, so also he casts out of the sacred enclosures those who confuse both the natures, and speak of their mixture and alteration: these are the Severuses and Eutycheses, and Dioscoruses, 3284Apollinariuses and Polemons, and before these the Simons, Valentinuses, Marcions, Basilideses, Maneses and Menanders, and the whole many-headed heresy of the Acephali, now wickedly stirring things up, which has one war, and that common, against piety, but carries on countless wars against itself that never cease. These, then, the truly holy and all-holy good tidings, and worthy of the inspired ears of us the pious, welcome us sweetly, but drive those foul and mad ones out of this festival of their good tidings, as men who understand and speak of them impiously.

48. But I, the least, who have come forward today as herald of these things, to you alone, the children of piety and faith, who receive piously the things said, cry with a great voice, and proclaim and utter expressly: «Good tidings, good tidings, good tidings.»

Οὐκέτι φθορὰν δεδιττόμενοι· οὐκέτι πάθη φοβούμενοι· οὐκέτι θάνατον τρέμοντες· οὐκέτι τὸν μιαρὸν ὀῤῥωδοῦντες διάβολον· οὐκέτι τὰς ἀφανεῖς αὐτοῦ προσβολὰς εὐλαβούμενοι· οὐκέτι τὰς φανερὰς αὐτοῦ τυραννίδας δειμαίνοντες· ἀλλ᾿ ὅλοι θεοὶ φαινόμενοι, ὅλοι θεοειδεῖς εὑρισκόμενοι, ὅλοι τῆς ἄνω χορείας Cod. χορίαςγραφόμενοι, ὅλοι πολῖταιτῆς 3285ἄνω πόλεως Ἰερουσαλὴμ θεωρούμενοι. Ἔνθα γὰρ ἂν σχῶμεν ἡμῶν τὸ πολίτευμα, ἐκεῖ καὶ τοὺς κλήρους δεξόμεθα, καὶ τὰς ἀῤῥήτους μονὰς κατοικήσομεν Cod. κατοικήσωμεν..

ΜΘʹ. Τούτων ἁπάντων, ἀδελφοὶ, τὰ εὐαγγέλια φέροντες, πρὸς τοὺς φιλτάτους ὑμᾶς ἀφικόμεθα τήμερον. Τούτων γὰρ ἁπάντων ἀρχὴ καὶ ῥίζα καὶ κρηπὶς καὶ θεμέλιος τὰ νῦν πρὸς ὑμᾶς εὐαγγέλια πέφυκεν. Ἐκ τούτων γὰρ, ὡς ἐμάθετε, πᾶσα ἡμῖν τῶν ἀγαθῶν ἡ ἀφθονία πηγάζει, ὥσπερ ἀπὸ πηγῆς τινος ἀναβλύζουσα, καὶ πᾶσαν ὁμοῦ τὴν οἰκουμένην ἀρδεύουσα, καὶ εὐφραίνουσα καὶ ἐκτρέφουσα, καὶ εἰς οὐρανίους αὐλὰς ἀναφέρουσα, καὶ τῶν ἐκεῖσε θειοτάτων μονῶν καὶ μακαρίων δωρεῶν κοινωνοὺς ἀποφαίνουσα.Καὶ διὰ τοῦτο πολλάκις βοῶ γεγηθὼς διαπρύσιον· «Εὐαγγέλια, εὐαγγέλια, εὐαγγέλια·» πρὸς ἅπερ ὑμᾶς καλῶ καὶ προτρέπομαι, καὶ ἅπερ ὑμᾶς ἑορτάζειν βεβούλημαι σήμερον. Ἔνθεν γὰρ ἡμῶν ἡ σωτηρία προέρχεται· ἔνθεν ἡμῖν ἡ ἐλευθερία· πυρσεύεται· ἔνθεν ἡμῶν ἡ πρὸς Θεὸν υἱοθεσία μαιεύεται· ἔνθεν ἡμῶν ἡ ἀνάκλησις τίκτεται· ἔνθεν ἡμᾶς ὁ Λόγος σώζειν καὶ Θεὸς ἀφικόμενος τῆς σωτηρίας ἀπάρχεται. Ταύτην τῆς εἰς ἡμᾶς εὐσπλαγχνίας ποιεῖται προοίμιον· ταύτην τῆς ἡμῶν θεουργίας τὴν ἀρχὴν ἐπεδείξατο· ἧς ἀνθρώποις οὐδὲν θυμηρέστερον· ἧς γηΐνοις οὐδὲν τιμαλφέστερον· ἧς τοῖς θνητοῖς οὐδὲν χαριέστερον. Ὅθεν ὁ δεύτερος Ἀδὰμ, τὴν παρθένον γῆν προσλαβόμενος, ἀναμορφώσας ἑαυτὸν ἀνθρωπίνῳ τῷ σχήματι, δευτέραν ἀρχὴν τῇ ἀνθρωπότητι τίθεται, τὴν τῆς προτέρας ἀνακαινίζων παλαίωσιν, καὶ δευτέραν ἡμῖν δεικνὺς ἀπαλαίωτον, τὸ μὲν συγγενὲς πρὸς ἐκείνην καὶ ὁμόφυλον ἔχουσαν, οὐ φέρουσαν δὲ πρὸς αὐτὴν τῆς ζωῆς τὸ ὁμότροπον, οὔτε τῆς ἀξίας καὶ δόξης ἱερᾶς τὸ ἰσόῤῥόπον. Ἐκείνη διὰ τὸν πρῶτον Ἀδὰμ κατεκρίνετο· αὕτη διὰ τὸν δεύτερον Ἀδὰμ ἠλευθέρωται μέμψεως. Ἐκείνη διὰ τὸν πρῶτον Ἀδὰμ, θεϊκῶν αὐγῶν ἐξωρφάνισται· αὕτη διὰ τὸν δεύτερον Ἀδὰμ, οὐρανίων αὐγῶν ἀναπέπλασται. Ἐκείνη διὰ τὸν πρῶτον Ἀδὰμ, τὸν ἐκ γῆς χοϊκὸν καὶ χοόφρονα, δουλείας ζυγὸν ὑπεβάσταξεν· αὕτη διὰ τὸν δεύτερον Ἀδὰμ καὶ Θεοῦ Υἱὸν ὁμοούσιον, υἱοθεσίας ἀξίωμα δέχεται· καὶ, «Ἀββᾶ, ὁ Πατὴρ,» κράζειν καὶ βοᾷν κατεπλούτησεν.

Νʹ. Ἆρα μὴ μικρὰ ἡμῶν, ἀδελφοὶ, τὰ εὐαγγέλια; ἆρα μὴ εὐτελὴς τῶν ἡμετέρων εὐαγγελίων ἡ κήρυξις; ἆρα μὴ ταπεινὴ καὶ κατάπτυστος Nic. pag. 242 omitt. καὶ κατάπτυστος. τῶν ἡμῶν εὐαγγελίων ἡ Nic. omitt. ἡ. ἔκλαμψις;

Good tidings of our Saviour, good tidings of God incarnate for our sakes; good tidings of the coming of God to us; good tidings of the becoming man of God, that from us; good tidings of the seedless conception of God; good tidings of the virginal child-bearing of God; good tidings of the unchangeable concourse of God with flesh; good tidings, in which we the outcasts are exalted and deified and become gods by grace: no longer dreading corruption; no longer fearing passions; no longer trembling at death; no longer shrinking from the foul devil; no longer wary of his unseen assaults; no longer afraid of his manifest tyrannies; but all appearing gods, all found godlike, all enrolled in the choir above, all beheld as 3285citizens of the city above, Jerusalem. For where we have our citizenship, there shall we also receive the inheritances, and dwell in the ineffable mansions.

49. Bearing the good tidings of all these things, brethren, we have come to you, most dear, today. For of all these the beginning and root and foundation and groundwork are the good tidings now brought to you. For from these, as you have learned, all the abundance of good things wells up for us, gushing forth as from some spring, and watering the whole inhabited world together, and gladdening and nourishing it, and bearing it up to heavenly courts, and rendering it partaker of the most divine mansions there and of blessed gifts. And for this reason I often cry piercingly, rejoicing: «Good tidings, good tidings, good tidings;» to which I call and exhort you, and which I have willed you to keep as a feast today. For hence our salvation proceeds; hence freedom is kindled for us like a beacon; hence our adoption to God is brought to birth; hence our recall is born; hence the Word and God, having come to save us, makes the beginning of salvation. This he makes the prelude of his compassion towards us; this he showed forth as the beginning of our deification; than which nothing is more heart-pleasing to men; than which nothing is more precious to the earthly; than which nothing is more gracious to mortals.

Whence the second Adam, taking to himself the virgin earth, reshaping himself in the human form, lays down a second beginning for manhood, renewing the oldness of the former, and showing us a second that does not grow old, which has indeed kinship and sameness of stock with that one, but does not bear towards it sameness in manner of life, nor equality in the weight of worth and sacred glory. That one, through the first Adam, was condemned; this one, through the second Adam, has been freed from blame. That one, through the first Adam, was orphaned of divine rays; this one, through the second Adam, has been refashioned with heavenly rays. That one, through the first Adam, the earthy from earth and earthly-minded, bore the yoke of slavery; this one, through the second Adam, the consubstantial Son of God, receives the dignity of adoption; and has been enriched to cry and to shout, «Abba, Father.»

50. Are our good tidings, then, brethren, small? Is the proclamation of our good tidings cheap? Is the shining-forth of our good tidings lowly and contemptible?

ἢ οὐ πᾶσαν ὑπερβαίνει 3288λαμπρότητα, καὶ πᾶσαν ὑπεραίρει φαιδρότητα, καὶ πᾶσαν ἀποκρύπτει κομψότητα; καὶ διὰ τοῦτο βοῶ πρὸς τοὺς κοινωνοὺς ὑμᾶς καὶ Nic. omitt. καί. ὁμόφρονας· καὶ «Εὐαγγέλια, εὐαγγέλια, εὐαγγέλια,» βοῶν οὐ κορέννυμαι. Ἔτι τρέχειν βουλόμενον καὶ καλῶς εὐδρομεῖν ἐφιέμενον, ἐπέχω τὸν λόγον. Κεκμηκότας γὰρ ὁρῶ τοὺς ἀκούοντας, καὶ ταῦτα μὴ κορεσθέντας τῆς τῶν ἱερῶν εὐαγγελίων ἡμῶν ἀκροάσεως. Τὸ μὲν γὰρ πνεῦμα πρόθυμον ἔχετε, ἀλλ᾿ ἀσθενῆ τὴν σάρκα· ἐπεὶ καὶ λόγου κόρον περίκεισθε, καὶ ἐπὶ πλεῖον μοχθεῖν οὐκ ἰσχύετε. Οἶδα δὲ ταῖς ἀκοαῖς καὶ λόγου κόρον πολέμιον, κἂν θεῖος ᾖ καὶ ὑπέρτατος, καὶ μάλιστα ταῖς ἁψικόρως πρὸς λόγων ἐχούσαις ἀκρόασιν. Ἱερῶς τοίνυν καὶ ὑψηλῶς τῶν ὑψηλῶν ἡμῶν εὐαγγελίων καὶ ἱερῶν τὴν ὑψηλὴν καὶ ἱερὰν ἑορτὴν ἑορτάσωμεν· καὶ ἀξίως αὐτὴν καὶ πρεπόντως τιμήσωμεν· ἵνα δι᾿ αὐτῆς ἀμαχότατα τῶν οὐρανίων ἀγαθῶν ἐπιτύχωμεν· ὧν ἡμῖν αὕτη ῥίζα καὶ πηγὴ καὶ γεννήτρια πέφηνεν, ἐν αὐτῷ Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ τῷ Κυρίῳ ἡμῶν, μεθ᾿ οὗ τῷ Πατρὶ καὶ τῷ ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι δόξα, τιμὴ, κράτος, μεγαλωσύνη, εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας τῶν αἰώνων. Ἀμήν.

Or does it not surpass all 3288brightness, and exceed all radiance, and obscure all elegance? And for this reason I cry to you, the partakers and like-minded; and crying «Good tidings, good tidings, good tidings,» I am not sated. Though my speech wishes still to run, and longs to run its course well, I hold it back. For I see the hearers weary, and that though not sated with the hearing of our sacred good tidings. For the spirit you have willing, but the flesh weak; since you are beset with satiety of speech, and have not strength to toil further. And I know that satiety of speech is hostile to the ears, even if the speech be divine and supreme, and most of all to those who in hearing speeches are quickly sated. Sacredly, then, and loftily let us keep the lofty and sacred feast of our lofty and sacred good tidings; and let us honour it worthily and fittingly; that through it we may most unassailably attain the heavenly goods, of which it has appeared to us root and source and begetter, in Christ Jesus our Lord himself, with whom to the Father and the Holy Spirit be glory, honour, might, majesty, to the ages of ages. Amen.

3287III.

Oratio de Hypapante sive occursu Domini,

Ex ms. Græco Mediceo Regis Franciæ. Edid. Bollandist. Act. Sanct., Februar. t. I, p. 274.

Cap. I. Mysteria Christi celebranda. Hujus festi solemnitas.

1. Aliud Christi est hoc mysterium, alia hæc est Christi Dei nostri magnificentia. Magna enim semper sunt illius miracula, ut quæ orationum aditus accessusquæ vincant omnium, omnisque excedant virtutem sapientiæ, non humanæ modo, sed etiam angelicarum, earumque que sub aspectum non cadunt, Virtutum. Sermo namque et lingua quædam, qui a nobis hominibus comprehendi nequeat, cœlestibus angelis seorsum data est. Quapropter et magna voce cum magno Paulo exclamabo: Ubi sapiens? Ubi scriba? Ubi conquisitor hujus sæculi I Cor. i, 20.? Ubi rhetorum ornamenta? Ubi philosophorum problemata? Ubi grammaticorum facetæ artes?

3287ON THE HYPAPANTE, OR THE MEETING OF THE LORD From a Medicean Greek manuscript of the King of France. Edited by the Bollandists, Acta Sanctorum, February, vol. I, p. 274.

Chapter 1. The mysteries of Christ to be celebrated. The solemnity of this feast.

1. This is another mystery of Christ, this another magnificence of Christ our God. For his miracles are always great, so that they overcome the approaches and accesses of the orations of all, and exceed the power of all wisdom, not of human wisdom only but also of the angelic, and of those Virtues which do not fall under sight. For a certain speech and tongue, which cannot be comprehended by us men, has been given apart to the heavenly angels. Wherefore I too will cry out with a great voice with the great Paul: «Where is the wise? Where is the scribe? Where is the disputer of this world?» I Cor. i, 20. Where are the ornaments of the rhetors? Where the problems of the philosophers? Where the elegant arts of the grammarians?

Certe stultam fecit Deus sapientiam hujus mundi, et supervacaneam vanamque, et inutilem vel ideo magis demonstravit, quia hæc sapientia donum etiam ac munus gratiæ erat, ad 3288observationem inquisitionemque Dei, vanoque ex errore resipiscendum, incredulis gentibus data. Verum «quia per mundi sapientiam non cognovit mundus Deum, placuit Deo per stultitiam prædicationis salvos facere credentes I Cor. i, 21. .» Et hæc jure eum Paulo clamo, ipseque etiam nunc admirabundus exclamabo : « O altitudo divitiarum et sapientiæ et scientiæ Dei ? Quam incomprehensibilia sunt judicia ejus, et investigabiles viæ ejus? Nullus enim cognovit sensum Domini, neque quisquam consiliarius ejus fuit Rom. xi, 33, 34. . Nam quo modo res creata mentem videat Conditoris, vel ingenium intellectumque illius possit comprehendere? Vel quo modo figmentum det consilium ei, qui ipsum finxerit, quasi committenti se figmenti consilio, indigentique imperfecto ad perfectionem ? Ideo apud insipientes, quod prædicatur, stultitia est: quia sapientia hominum infatuata est.

Surely God has made foolish the wisdom of this world, and has shown it superfluous and vain, and useless — and the more for this very reason, that this wisdom too was a gift and a boon of grace, given to the unbelieving nations for 3288the observation and inquiry of God, and for coming to their senses from vain error. But «because in the wisdom of the world the world knew not God, it pleased God by the foolishness of preaching to save them that believe.» I Cor. i, 21. And these things I rightly cry with Paul, and, wondering still even now, I will exclaim: «O the depth of the riches and of the wisdom and of the knowledge of God! How incomprehensible are his judgments, and his ways unsearchable! For no one has known the mind of the Lord, nor has anyone been his counsellor.» Rom. xi, 33, 34. For how should a created thing see the mind of the Maker, or be able to comprehend his genius and understanding? Or how should the thing formed give counsel to him who formed it, as though he committed himself to the counsel of the thing formed, and stood in need of the imperfect for perfection? Therefore among the unwise what is preached is foolishness, because the wisdom of men has been made foolish.

Ideo Academia obdormiit, Stoa Schola Stoicorum et Peripateticorum. conticuit, vacat Peripatus, dormit 3289Lyceum Gymnasium Athenis, in quo docuit Aristoteles. , Athenæ proculcatæ sunt: ut ne, relicto Deo omnium Conditore, deos faciant res ab ipso creatas. Hi enim contra Deum Deique militant sapientiam, qui ea quæ videntur, Deo esse majora judicant, neque Deum eorumdem Conditorem videre cupiunt. Propterea tradita ea silentio sunt, atque in profundum oblivionis projecta.

2. Hinc potius Nazareth praedicatur, in qua Evangelia Dei recitata sunt : prænuntiatur hinc Bethlehem, ubi Deus in carne nascitur Desunt nonnulla, potissimum de urbe Hierosolymitana. .. . . ubi Deus miracula patrans vitam degit. Præsignatur hinc Golgotha, ubi Deus crucem suscipit : hinc canitur resurrectio, in qua e sepulcro Deus rursum exsurgit Psal. xlvii, 3. : prædicatur hinc Sion, ac latera Aquilonis presignificantur, ubi Christus, cum a mortuis resurrexisset, apparet : hinc gloria mons Olivarum afficitur, e quo cum humana natura in cœlum Deus revertitur. Ablata sunt priora, atque alia introducta : infatuata illa, hæc demonstrata sunt: illa in cinerem redacta, hæc illustrata : illa juste, quia Deum non honoravere, contempta sunt; convenienter hæc, quia Deo, ut oportebat, dedere gloriam, vicissim glorie commendata sunt. Illa enim hominibus erant offensioni, trahebant ad interitum, in inferni barathrum deferebant ; ast hæc hominibus et saluti sunt, eosque e morte ad vitam reducunt, et e terra in cœlum revehunt. Illis in oblivionem actis, hæc festo jam colimus : hæc hodierna die universi concelebramus : his, ut par est, initiamur, Dei enim festa sunt, atque mysteria , quæ, cum juxta hominum conditionem apte perficiuntur, tum mystice sese perficientem ipsa perficiunt.

3. At quod nunc etiam hoc mysterium sit (hujus enim est præsens hæc celebritas) vel quæ hæc Christi magnificentia (hujus enim est hæc pompa atque progressus) continuo nos roget quispiam, vel infidelitate detentus, vel etiam ignorantiam religionis affectans, Ecclesiæ namque filiis, initiatisque a sancto Spiritu, omnia, quæ Christum attinent, manifesta sunt atque cognita, nihilque est eorum, qui peraguntur, ignotum. Ego vero et clamabo, atque eloquar, prædicaboque, quæ festi hujus sunt propria. « Non enim erubesco Evangelium Christi, » quod clamavit ante nos Paulus, sed extraneorum sapientum per ipsum fastus pudore suffundo.

Therefore the Academy has fallen asleep, the Stoa has fallen silent, the Peripatos stands empty, the Lyceum 3289sleeps, Athens has been trodden underfoot: so that they should not, forsaking God the Maker of all, make gods of the things created by him. For these wage war against God and the wisdom of God, which judge that the things which are seen are greater than God, and do not wish to see God their Maker. Therefore they have been consigned to silence, and cast into the depth of oblivion.

2. Hence rather Nazareth is proclaimed, in which the good tidings of God were recited; hence Bethlehem is foretold, where God is born in the flesh […] where God, working miracles, passed his life. Hence Golgotha is foreshown, where God takes up the cross; hence the resurrection is sung, in which God rises again from the tomb; hence Sion is proclaimed, and the sides of the north are foreshown, Psal. xlvii, 3. where Christ, when he had risen from the dead, appears; hence the Mount of Olives is touched with glory, from which God returns to heaven with the human nature. The former things have been taken away, and others brought in; those made foolish, these shown forth; those reduced to ashes, these made illustrious; those justly despised, because they did not honour God; these fittingly, because they gave God glory as was due, commended in turn to glory. For those were a stumbling to men, drew them to destruction, bore them down into the pit of hell; but these are salvation to men, and lead them back from death to life, and carry them up from earth to heaven. Those having been driven into oblivion, these we now worship with a feast; these on this present day we all celebrate together; into these, as is meet, we are initiated. For they are feasts and mysteries of God, which, as they are fittingly performed according to the condition of men, so they themselves mystically perfect him who performs them.

3. But what this mystery now is (for the present celebration is of it), or what this magnificence of Christ is (for this pomp and procession is of it), someone may at once ask us, either held by unbelief, or even affecting ignorance of religion; for to the sons of the Church, and to those initiated by the Holy Spirit, all things that concern Christ are manifest and known, and nothing of the things that are performed is unknown. But I will both cry aloud, and speak out, and proclaim, the things that are proper to this feast. «For I am not ashamed of the Gospel of Christ,» which Paul cried before us; but through it I cover with shame the pride of the wise who are outside. «For the power of God, which cannot be comprehended, is unto salvation to everyone that believes.» Rom. i, 16. And indeed how could it come about that I should blush to bring forth the ineffable might of the Godhead?

Virtus enim Dei, quae comprehendi nequit, est in salutem omni credenti Rom. i, 16. » Et vero qui possit fieri, ut potentiam Numinis ineffabilem proferre erubescam ? mysteria nostra frustra ne incusent Aristagoræ De his philosophis agit Laertius in libris De Vitis philosophorum, et passim antiqui. , Anaxagoræ, Anaximandri, Pythagoræ. 3290Aristoteles, Platones, sapientiæ jam corruptæ sapientissimi defensores, qui quæ dicunt nesciunt, eaque quæ asserunt intelligere nequeunt, quos merito et piscatorius Apostolos Petrum et Paulum intelligit : ita eosdem S. Chrysostomus aliique Patres describunt. nota perstrinxit calamus, et artis sutoriæ scalpellum demessuit mortuaque et insensilia ostendit esse terræ retrimenta . verum his e sapiente nostra celebritate dimissis (quippe qui Dei sapientiam et ex se ipsi expulerunt, et inani se vanaque stultitia repleverunt) nos ore cuncti theologico miranda atque a Deo recte facta accinamus, purisque mentibus celebremus. Hoc enim et divinis Dei festis conducit maxime, atque hic eos, qui festa sua concelebrent, requirit.

4. Natus est æternus ille Christus in carne, ac more nostro sibi matrem sumpsit, quæ ipsum indubitate et ineffabiliter genuit : atque hanc nos, qui salutem ex ipsa decerpsimus, mystice nuper festo die coluimus. Post quem rite octavus dies exspectatam carnis circumcisionem excepit : tum vero tanquam mystæ clientesque Dei, quique divina Dei dona acceperimus, rursum mystice hanc peregimus. Aliud autem nunc succedit illis mysterium alioque mortalibus dono gratificatur. Nihil enim a Deo nato proficiscitur, quin ad hominum utilitatem omnino referatur : præ desiderio certe humiles abjectosque extollendi, nostra hominum causa natus est. Quodnam autem nunc etiam illud est, quod ut Deum decet, celebramus mysterium ? Progressus Christi perquam admirabilis, adventus Christi felicitate plenissimus, Christi perdifficilis explicatu oblatio, Christi e divinissimo quidem Bethleemo excedentis, adventantis autem hanc in urbem Hierosolymam Græce ταύτην τὴν πόλιν, ubi se degere indicat. , de loco in locum transgressio.

5. Ecquis potest hanc Dei ex alio in alium locum migrationem effari ? Quapropter, O viri, eadem nobiscum sapientia repleti, ineffabilia Jesu Christi mysteria, atque ejusmodi ut nequeat ea flumen orationis complecti. Quomodo enim Dei non circumscripti progressum, ejusdemque ex aliis in alia loca transgressionem audiens, non continuo ridebit dicentem, tanquam incredibilia in hominum mentes nulloque pacto recipienda inducentem ?

Let them not accuse our mysteries in vain, the Aristagorases, the Anaxagorases, the Anaximanders, the Py3290thagorases, the Aristotles, the Platos, most wise defenders of a wisdom now corrupted, who do not know what they say, and cannot understand what they assert; whom with good reason both the fisherman's reed has censured with its mark, and the knife of the leather-cutter's craft has mown down, and has shown them to be dead and senseless offscourings of the earth. But these being dismissed from our wise celebration (seeing that they have both driven the wisdom of God out of themselves, and filled themselves with an empty and vain foolishness), let us all, with a theological mouth, sing of the wonders and the things rightly done by God, and celebrate them with pure minds. For this is most of all fitting to the divine feasts of God, and he requires this of those who celebrate his feasts.

4. That eternal Christ was born in the flesh, and after our manner took to himself a mother, who bore him indubitably and ineffably; and her we, who have gathered salvation from her, lately honoured mystically on a festal day. After which the eighth day duly received the expected circumcision of the flesh; and then, as initiates and clients of God, and as those who have received the divine gifts of God, we again mystically celebrated this. But now another mystery succeeds those, and gratifies mortals with another gift. For nothing proceeds from God born that is not wholly referred to the advantage of men: surely from desire of lifting up the humble and abject, he was born for the sake of us men. But what is this mystery too, which now, as befits God, we celebrate? The procession of Christ, most admirable; the coming of Christ, most full of felicity; the offering of Christ, most difficult to explain; the passing from place to place of Christ, going forth from most divine Bethlehem, and arriving in this city of Jerusalem.

5. Who can tell forth this migration of God from one place into another? Wherefore, O men filled with the same wisdom as we, ineffable are the mysteries of Jesus Christ, and of such a kind that the river of speech cannot embrace them. For how will one, hearing of the procession of the uncircumscribed God, and of the passing of the same from some places into others, not at once laugh at the speaker, as one bringing into the minds of men things incredible and in no way to be received?

D Prodit tamen incircumscriptus (reprehendant licet gentiles hoc praeconium, insipientia ejus notata), prodit qui naturalem ambitum (quippe Deus) non sustinet, imo, qui natura potentiaque infinitus est, (finita enim hæc natura est finitæque virtutis) nostro hominum more prodit : rumpatur licet, qui Filium ait esse alium, qui processerit, in alioque 3291delirat, qui non processerit, atque ita filiorum Christorumque par nobis somniat miser ille Nestorius. Ille vero potius et ex se sibi persuadet impie, et Christi mysterium nefando ausu dilacerat, Voluit enim procedere duntaxat natum ex Virgine, non autem natum ex Patre Filium, Verbum illud sempiternum. At verum manifestumque Deus fecit progressum. Vere enim erat caro factus et corpus, comprehensusque fuerat circumscriptione corporea : exasperetur licet is, qui naturam carnis in Verbo neget, quod caro factum est, progressionemque merum spectrum fuisse somniat, miser Eutyches. Si enim Verbi, quod caro factum est natura mansisset unica, fuisset spectrum, quod apparebat, et nuda umbra hoc Christi esset mysterium, nec vere Verbum incarnatum. Etenim quomodo Verbum, si non assumpsit naturam carnis, immutabiliter caro factum est ? At Verbum revera caro factum est, conversionemque in carnem nullo modo sustinuit; inviolataque ex Virgine accepit naturam carnis, secundum quam ex ea etiam est progenitum. Idem et dissolutionem non admittit naturarum, et in naturis innotescit duabus : neque ob differentiam earum divisum, quemadmodum insanus furebat Nestorius : neque confundens substantiam Græce, οὔτε συγχέων τὴν ἕνωσιν (potius οὐσίαν) τῶν ἐξ ὧν ἡ καθ᾽ ὑπόστασιν γέγονεν ἕνωσις. , quorum in hypostasi facta est conjunctio. Ita unus idemque et in eodem exsistens, Deus innotescit et homo, exhibet personam unicam, constitutam, ex ambabus constantem naturis; attamen, quæ erant ambarum propria, non confundentem : ex quibus enim immutabiliter componebatur, indivise in iisdem apparet, nec mysterium confundens suum, neque suam personam dividens.

Cap. II. Occursus Christi. Lumina collata. Dies purgationis.

6. Ita coelestis ille Bethlemita procedit : ita apparet aspectabilis, qui fugit aciem oculorum : ita expers ille corporis in carne monstratur : ita venit circumscriptus, qui circumscribi non potest, At in occursum curramus ejus universi, qui tam pie illius colimus veneramurque mysterium, propenso cuncti animo eamus. Quis illi primus occurret? Quis Deum oculis cernet primus? quis Deum primus excipiet ? Quis primus ulnis portabit Deum?

Yet the uncircumscribed goes forth (let the gentiles find fault with this proclamation, its folly marked), he goes forth who does not endure natural bounds (since he is God), nay, he who is infinite in nature and in power (for this nature is finite, and of finite power) goes forth after the manner of us men: let him burst, who says that the Son who went forth is one, and — he 3291raves — another who did not go forth, and so that wretch Nestorius dreams up for us a pair of sons and of Christs. He rather both persuades himself of it out of himself, impiously, and with unspeakable daring tears the mystery of Christ apart. For he would have the one born of the Virgin alone go forth, and not the Son born of the Father, that everlasting Word. But God made a true and manifest procession. For he had truly been made flesh and body, and had been comprehended by bodily circumscription: let him be exasperated who denies the nature of flesh in the Word, which was made flesh, and dreams that the procession was a mere phantom — the wretched Eutyches. For if the nature of the Word which was made flesh had remained single, what appeared would have been a phantom, and this mystery of Christ would be a bare shadow, and the Word not truly incarnate. For how was the Word made flesh without change, if it did not assume the nature of flesh? But the Word was in very truth made flesh, and in no way underwent conversion into flesh; and it received from the inviolate Virgin the nature of flesh, according to which it was also begotten from her. The same both admits no dissolution of the natures, and is made known in two natures: not divided on account of their difference, as the mad Nestorius raved; nor confounding the essence of those whose union was made in hypostasis. So one and the same, and existing in the same, is made known as God and man, displays a single person, constituted, consisting of both natures; yet not confounding the things that were proper to both: for in those from which he was compounded without change, he appears without division, neither confounding his mystery nor dividing his person.

Chapter 2. The meeting of Christ. The lights brought together. The day of purification.

6. So that heavenly one of Bethlehem goes forth; so he appears visible, who escapes the keenness of the eyes; so he who is without body is shown in the flesh; so he comes circumscribed, who cannot be circumscribed. But let us all run to meet him, who so piously honour and venerate his mystery; let us all go with an eager mind. Who will meet him first? Who will first behold God with his eyes? Who will first receive God? Who will first carry God in his arms?

Neminem pigeat cursus accelerati, nemo ad gressum concitatum claudicet, nemo pium abrumpat exitum, nemo ad divinum occursum tardet : nemo celebritatis degustatæ spectetur expers, nemo mysteriorum exsors appareat , nemo lucifera hilaritate privetur : nemo ad cursum Simeonis senis velocissimum fiat languidior, nemo anu illa Anna pede tardior judicetur, ut ne quo modo senes vereque grandævi illum, quem deficientem 3292viderint pedibus, citoque cursu destitui, non solum incusent, sed remissum etiam animum, fortasse et infidelem, eidem propheticis verbis exprobrent. Nemo igitur sit occursus non particeps, nemo ab luce Græce φωτοφορίας ἀλλότριος, allusione ad cereos qui ferebantur, facta. ferenda alienus.

7. Cereorum etiam splendorem addimus, tum ostendendo splendorem accedentis divinum, a quo cuncta splendescunt, pessimisque depulsis tenebris illustrantur abundantia lucis æternæ ; tum vel maxime manifestando splendorem animæ, quicum nos occurrere Christo necesse sit. Ut enim Dei Mater Virgoque integerrima, veram ulnis tulit lucem, iisque adfuit, qui jacebant in tenebris ; ita et illustrationibus illius nos illustrati, ac lumen omnibus apparens tenentes manibus, ei qui vere lux est, obviam ire festinemus. Nam profecto, quia lux venit in mundum Joan. iii, 19. , eumque tenebris circumfusum illustravit, et quia visitavit nos Oriens ex alto, atque his qui in tenebris sedebant, affulsit Luc. i, 78. ; hoc nostrum est mysterium ; idcirco lampades tenentes incedimus, idcirco lumina ferentes accurrimus, tum ita designantes, quod illuxit nobis, lumen ; tum splendorem, ab eo nobis affuturum, innuentes. Quapropter curramus una, Deo cuncti occurramus ; ne, si facere id piguerit, vel de animo ab eo ingrato aliquando accusemur, vel de metuendo ipsius contempti crimine : audiamusque, quod etiam dicentem eum prius audierant tenebris illis involuti, nec illustrandi Judæi: « Quia Lux venit in mundum, et dilexerunt homines magis tenebras, quam lucem Joan. iii, 19. : erant enim eorum mala opera (semper enim animum obscurat malitia, nec eum sinit videre lucem) : vel etiam illa (quod sacrum ait Evangelium) « Lux in tenebris lucet, et tenebræ eam non comprehenderunt Joan. i, 5. » Lux ergo illa vero, que illuminat omnem hominem venientem in hunc mundum Joan. i, 9. , venit. Omnes ergo, o fratres, illuminemur; omnes illustremur. Nemo nostrum maneat hoc non initiatus splendore, nemo oppletus illum nocte persistat ; sed prodeamus omnes splendidi: illustrati eamus obviam simul universi, lucemque illam claram ac sempiternam excipiamus cum sene Simeone: exsultantesque cum eo animis, Genitori Patrique lucis, qui misit. lucem veram, caliginemque depulit, ac nos omnes effecit splendidos, cum gratiarum actione hymnum concinamus. Dei enim salutare, quod paravit ante faciem omnium populorum atque ad nostram novi Israelis gloriam manifestavit, etiam nos per ipsum vidimus, fuimusque veteri illo obscuroque crimine, uti vitæ præsentis vinculis viso Christo Simeon Luc. ii, 31., confestim absoluti.

Let no one be loath of a hastened course, let no one limp at a quickened step, let no one break off the pious going-out, let no one be slow [?] to the divine meeting; let no one be seen without part in the celebration once tasted, no one appear without share in the mysteries, no one be deprived of the light-bearing gladness; let no one grow more sluggish at the most swift running of the old man Simeon, no one be judged slower of foot than that aged woman Anna; lest in any way the old men, truly of great age, should not only reproach him whom they see failing in his feet 3292and left behind in the swift course, but should also upbraid him with prophetic words for a slack mind, perhaps an unbelieving one too. Let no one, then, be without share in the meeting, no one a stranger to the carrying of the light.

7. We add also the splendour of candles, both showing the divine splendour of him who approaches, from whom all things shine, and, the worst darkness driven away, are illumined by the abundance of eternal light; and, most of all, manifesting the splendour of the soul with which we must meet Christ. For as the Mother of God and most pure Virgin carried the true light in her arms, and came to those who lay in darkness; so we too, illumined by his illuminations, and holding in our hands the light that appears to all, let us hasten to go to meet him who is truly light. For indeed, because the light came into the world, and illumined it, wrapped round with darkness, Joan. iii, 19. and because the Dayspring from on high has visited us, and has shone upon those who sat in darkness; Luc. i, 78. this is our mystery; for this reason we walk holding lamps, for this reason we run up carrying lights, both so signifying the light that has shone upon us, and hinting at the splendour that will come to us from him. Wherefore let us run together, let us all meet God; lest, if we are loath to do it, we be accused by him one day either of an ungrateful mind, or of the fearful crime of having despised him; and lest we hear what the Jews too, wrapped in that darkness and not to be illumined, had heard him say before: «Because the light has come into the world, and men loved darkness rather than light: for their works were evil» Joan. iii, 19. (for malice always darkens the mind, and does not let it see the light); or even that (which the holy Gospel says): «The light shines in darkness, and the darkness comprehended it not.» Joan. i, 5. So that true light, which enlightens every man coming into this world, Joan. i, 9. has come. All of us, then, O brethren, let us be enlightened; let us all be illumined. Let none of us remain uninitiated in this splendour, none persist filled with that night; but let us all go forth shining; illumined, let us all go to meet him together, and receive that bright and everlasting light [?] with the old man Simeon: and, exulting with him in our souls, let us sing a hymn with thanksgiving to the Begetter and Father of lights, who sent the true light, and drove away the gloom, and made us all shining. For the salvation of God, which he prepared before the face of all peoples, and manifested for the glory of us, the new Israel, we too have seen through him, and have been at once released from that old and dark crime, as Simeon, when he had seen Christ, from the bonds of the present life.

Nos etiam Christum e Bethlehemo ad nos profectum complexi fide, facti sumus ex gentibus, Dei populus (ipse enim Dei Patrisque salutare est) oculisque Deum carnem factum conspeximus, 3293ac visa Dei præsentia ulnisque mentis admissa, novus Israel nuncupamur, eamque festis anniversariis, in oblivionem ejus nunquam venturi, celebramus.

8. Verum Christusne ubique præsens accedit Intelligi solam Christi deitatem infra patet, ubi hæretici de ea male sentientes perstringuntur. ? An lux implens omnia procedit in medium? Nihil enim illius, cum natura non circumscripta sit, nullaque abs re arceri valeat, velitque legem implere priscam, vacuum est claritate. Imo vero accedit, ortumque suum omnino summum præsignificans, de se Mosaico vaticinatus est ore. At quale hoc vaticinium ? « Omne masculinum adaperiens vulvam, inquit, sanctum Domino vocabitur Exod. xiii, 2 ; Luc. ii, 23. » Quod quidem præceptum, magis tamen denuntiationem propheticam, etiam solus implevit, cum ex utero Virginis solus ipse prospexit, eamque tamen virginitate non privavit sua, ejusdemque, cum procederet, vinculum non dissolvit : quod de solo ipso aperte prædictum erat. Nam et ipse solus e sola natus est Virgine, et a seminis experte vulva solus est genitus, et solus e Virginis utero progressus, ipsam Virginem conservavit, non corrupta ejus gratia nascens, illius non minuto splendore in lucem veniens, vinculo illius non soluto progrediens : quo et Deum ostendunt eum esse, qui nascitur, et Deiparam declarant proprie eam esse quæ parit. Ubi scelestus iterum videtur esse Nestorius, qui neque Deum dicere vult eum qui nascitur; nec Deiparam eam sustinet vocare quæ peperit : ubi exsecrandus quoque Eutyches spectatur, qui carnem negat Domino ejusdem, atque nobis, esse nature. Quo enim modo Verbum, quod caro nostra fieri causa sustinuit, Caro factum est Joan. i, 14. » si natura carnis ab eo ex nobis neque vere assumpta, neque ita ipsi juncta erat, ut ostenderet liquido, eamdem, quam nos, naturam Verbum ante corporis expers suscepisse, atque ita vere hominem factum esse nobiscum, si neque habeat corpus ejusdem cum corporibus nostris generis, neque animam ejusdem, qua nostræ constant naturæ? At vere « Verbum caro factum, » nos servare cupit : et exsistens in ipso Deus vere factus homo, nihil falsi præsefert, neque homines nos decepit ; sed nostra natura suscepta gratificatus est nobis suam.

9. Etenim, si hoc ita non erat ut credimus, quomodo, qui hæc scribit, ait Evangelista: « Et postquam impleti sunt dies purgationis eorum Luc. ii, 22. ? » Qualis porro purgationis, o abominande Eutyches, si caro in Christo non erat vera ? si natura humana adunata non erat Verbo? nam divina expers corporis quali eget purgatione ? que contaminatio in experte carnis divinitate conspicitur ?

We too, having embraced by faith Christ, who set out from Bethlehem to us, have become, out of the nations, the people of God (for he is the salvation of God and the Father), and with our eyes we have 3293beheld God made flesh; and, the presence of God having been seen and admitted into the arms of the mind, we are called the new Israel; and this presence, never to come into forgetfulness of it, we celebrate in yearly feasts.

8. But does Christ, who is present everywhere, approach? Does the light that fills all things proceed into the midst? For nothing of his — since his nature is not circumscribed, and can be shut out from no thing, and he wills to fulfil the ancient law — is empty of brightness. Nay rather, he does approach, and, foreshowing his own wholly supreme birth, he prophesied of himself by the mouth of Moses. And what is this prophecy? «Every male that opens the womb,» it says, «shall be called holy to the Lord.» Exod. xiii, 2; Luc. ii, 23. Which precept indeed, but more a prophetic announcement, he alone fulfilled, when he alone looked forth from the womb of the Virgin, and yet did not deprive her of her virginity, and did not loose her bond as he came forth: which had been plainly foretold of him alone. For he alone was born of a Virgin alone, and alone was begotten from a womb without experience of seed, and alone, coming forth from the Virgin's womb, kept the Virgin herself: being born without corrupting her grace, coming into the light without lessening her splendour, going forth without loosing her bond: by which things they both show that he who is born is God, and declare that she who bears is properly Theotokos. Where the wicked Nestorius is seen again, who neither wishes to call him who is born God, nor endures to call her who bore him Theotokos: where the execrable Eutyches too is beheld, who denies to the Lord flesh of the same nature as ours. For in what way was the Word, which endured to become flesh for our sake, «made flesh», Joan. i, 14. if the nature of flesh was neither truly assumed by him from us, nor so joined to him as to show clearly that the Word, before without body, took up the same nature as we, and so truly became man with us — if he has neither a body of the same kind as our bodies, nor a soul the same as that of which our natures consist? But truly «the Word made flesh» wishes to save us: and God, existing in it, truly made man, displays nothing false, nor did he deceive us men; but, having taken up our nature, he graciously gave us his own.

9. For if this were not so as we believe, how does the Evangelist who writes these things say: «And after the days of their purification were fulfilled»? Luc. ii, 22. And of what purification, O abominable Eutyches, if the flesh in Christ was not true? if the human nature was not united to the Word? For the divine nature, without body, what purification does it need? What contamination is beheld in the Godhead without flesh?

Quanquam ut hominum conditio exigebat, humilius hoc conscriptum 3294est, vel maxime ob te amentiamque tuam imaginariam, atque instar Manichæorum attonitum stuporem tuum. Nam neque, qui omnium est expiator Christus, ulla expurgatione indigebat, ac cepta præsertim carne illa sola immaculata : neque quæ pºperit, inviolata illà impollutaque vere Virgo, quæ omnium genuit expiamentum, expiatione per legem præscripta eguit. Quanquam hoc, secundum legem, ascriptum sit, ut ne Christus dissolutor esse legis existimaretur.

Cap. III. Ad occursum admittuntur. orthodoxi, repelluntur hæretici.

10. Quid autem vel ipsa inviolatis gestavit ulnis, vel quid Simeon excepit suis, præter Deum carnem corpusque factum ? Et Deum affectu religioso propheta suscipiebat, ac Patri Deoque illum deferebat, et legem implebat spiritu, « Nunc,» gratis inquiens labiis, « dimittis servum tuum, Domine, secundum verbum tuum, in pace Luc. ii, 29. Quod ei ex Spiritu dictum erat ibid. 26. , non visurum se mortis aditum, nisi prius videret Christum Domini, legisque vinculum solveretur. Senex enim ille obtinebat personam legis. « Quia, inquit, viderunt oculi mei Salutare tuum ibid. 30. Non enim erat Salutare lex illa Mosaica. Lex autem vetus et senex et infirma, viso Christo, hoc est Salutari Dei, et ab infirmitate ad salutem reducebatur; et vetustate, quæ illi aderat, convenienter novitati renovata, eximebatur : lumen enim veterem Israel omni vetustate liberans, ad eumque gentes adducens, oculis cernebatur. Idcirco clamat, clamansque enuntiat : « Nunc dimittis servum tuum, Domine, secundum verbum tuum, in pace ; quia viderunt oculi mei Salutare tuum, quod parasti ante faciem omnium populorum, lumen ad revelationem gentium et gloriam plebis tuæ Israel ibid. 31.. » Exsistens novus e veteri (et Galilæa gentium illustratur, et novus Israel demonstratur) Christum, lumen, aspexit.

11. Quid autem vidit etiam oculis, nisi cognatum nobis corpus atque conspicuum, convincensque inscitiam Eutychetis e Manichaica semente productam? Ex natura enim sua fugit aspectum Deitas, nec hominum oculis cerni potest. Excedant ergo hac nostra celebritate simul Eutyches ac Nestorius ejusdem hostes et adversarii, illiusque lætitiam violare certantes : hic quidem Christum dividens individuum, ille vero impie ea, que confundi non possunt, confundens : nec enim unquam a talibus nostra se patitur adornari celebritas.

Although, as the condition of men demanded, this was 3294written in humbler wise, most of all on account of thee, and thy imaginary madness, and thy stupor, thunderstruck like that of the Manichaeans. For neither did Christ, who is the expiator of all, need any expurgation, especially as that flesh alone immaculate had been taken; nor did she who bore him, the truly inviolate and undefiled Virgin, who bore the expiation of all, need the expiation prescribed through the law. Although this was ascribed to them according to the law, lest Christ should be thought a dissolver of the law.

Chapter 3. The orthodox are admitted to the meeting; the heretics are repelled.

10. But what did she carry in her inviolate arms, or what did Simeon receive in his, but God made flesh and body? And the prophet both received God with religious affection, and offered him to the Father and God, and fulfilled the law in spirit, saying with grateful lips: «Now thou lettest thy servant depart, O Lord, according to thy word, in peace.» Luc. ii, 31. Which had been told him by the Spirit: that he would not see the approach of death unless first he saw the Christ of the Lord, and the bond of the law were loosed. ibid. 26. For that old man bore the person of the law. «For,» he says, «mine eyes have seen thy Salvation.» For that Mosaic law was not Salvation. But the law, old and aged and weak, when Christ was seen, that is, the Salvation of God, was both brought back from weakness to salvation; and, renewed fittingly by newness, was freed from the oldness that was in it: for the light that frees old Israel from all oldness, and leads the nations to him, was beheld with the eyes. Therefore he cries, and crying declares: «Now thou lettest thy servant depart, O Lord, according to thy word, in peace; for mine eyes have seen thy Salvation, which thou hast prepared before the face of all peoples, a light for the revelation of the gentiles, and the glory of thy people Israel.» Luc. ii, 29. Being new out of old (both Galilee of the nations is illumined, and the new Israel is shown forth), he beheld Christ, the light.

11. But what did he see with his eyes too, but a body akin to us and visible, and convicting the ignorance of Eutyches, produced from Manichaean seed? For by its own nature the Godhead escapes sight, and cannot be seen by the eyes of men. Let Eutyches and Nestorius, then, both go out from this our celebration, enemies and adversaries of the same, striving to violate its gladness: the one dividing Christ the undivided, the other impiously confounding the things that cannot be confounded; for never does our celebration suffer itself to be adorned by such men.

Idcirco purgamini, idcirco nitescite, idcirco Christi mysteria, velut Christo amici, colite ; etenim aliter sibi obviam accurrentes neque admittit, neque oculis videre sustinet : verum a celebritate sua 3295eos exsules esse jubet, profanosque constituit et occursus ad se exsortes. Abeant una cum his Eunomii et Eudoxii Eudoxius Arianus Constantinopolitanæ invasor sedis baptizavit Valentem imperatorem, cui se adjunxit Aetius magister Eunomii, dein ab Eudoxio constituti episcopi Cyziceni, quem expulsum apud se Constantinopoli detinuit. Ita Socr. l. IV, c. 1 et 7, et S. Epiphan. hæres. 75, n. 38, qui hæreticis Anomæis annumerat, quorum princeps Eunomius habebatur. et Asterii Asterius Sophista in persecutione Maximiani Christianam fidem ejuravit. S. Epiphan. hæres. 76, n. 3. Agunt de eo homine Cappadoce et Ariano, ejusque libris Socr. l. I, c. 24 ; Sozom. l. II, c. 31 ; S. Athanasius De Synodo, aliique. , et cum ipsis Manichæi et Apollinarii Apollinaris Laodiceni hæreses describit Epiphan. hæresi 77, n. 22 ; scilicet Christum corpus e cœlo detulisse, animam non assumpsisse, corpus divinitati consubstantiale fuisse. Agit de eo Sozomen. l. VI, c. 25, et alii : eum S. Damasus papa Romæ in synodo damnavit anno 373. , Severi ac Severus sedem Antiochenam expulso S. Flaviano per vim invasit anno 513, ex secta Acephalorum, oppugnator concil. Chalcedonensis. Coluntur SS. Flavianus, et Elias tunc quoque per ipsum e sede Hierosolymitana pulsus, 4 Julii. Dioscori Dioscorus episcopus Alexandrinus patronus Eutychetis, cum eo in concilio Chalcedonensi damnatus. De eo agemus 18 Febr. ad Vitam S. Flaviani episcopi Constantinopolitani, ab ejus factionis hominibus in exsilium pulsi. , omnisque Acephalorum Cum Petrus Moggus in sedem Alexandrinam anno 482 receptus, Henotico Zenonis imperatoris admisso, Catholicos simul et hæreticos offendisset, Eutychiani plures ab utrisque deficientes, Acephali, id est, sine capite sunt appellati : in varias mox sectas divisi. misera illa ac vere capite carens gangræna. Illi quidem quia ob carnem Christi conspicuam, et inaspectabilem perfectamque deitatem imminuere et abrogare conati sunt, eamque alterius efficere natura, substantiæque a natura, quæ est in Patre, qui genuit, contendere diversæ, in creatis eam rebus audacter numerantes, ac decernentes Christum Deum creatum esse, et, secundum utramque naturam, divinam, inquam, et humanam adorandum esse. Hi vero ob illam Christi, quae non videtur, deitatem, etiam carnem ejus eludere ementirique volunt : qui ea quæ unius solum nature propria sunt, abripientes, conjunctarum confusionem substantiarum imaginantur: et miseras Apollinarii Polemonisque Polemonem ex scriptis Apollinarii ansam nactum, dixisse in Christo substantiarum confusionem factam, et commistionem divinitatis et corporis tradit Theodoretus, l. IV Hæret. fabul. cap. 9. præstigias libenti animo accipiunt. Quibuscum Ebiones Ebion princeps hæresis, quæ Christum nudum hominem appellavit, contra quem et Cerinthum S. Joannes suum Evangelium scripsit. Agunt de eo Theodoretus in compendio hæreticarum fabularum, earumque lib. II, cap. 1 ; S. Epiphanius hæresi 51, aliique. ac Samosateni De Paulo Samosateno agit S. Ephræm Syrus in Testamento quo Bardesanem strenue confutatum esse tradit Theodoretus lib. I Hær. fab., cap. 22 ; de hoc etiam agit S. Epiphanius hæresi 22. , Pauli et Bardesanæ collocentur, nostroque e festo hoc exigantur. Hi quidem, quia Christum solummodo nudum hominem ac nobis parem arbitrati sunt, iisque que deitatis sunt propria, carentem, ac ne Deum quidem ullo modo nominaverunt. Illi vero, quod Christum non æternum esse Deum Patrique æternitate parem, sed ex Maria affirmabant solum ex ipsa ineffabili incarnatione Deum exstitisse rati.

12.

Therefore be purified, therefore shine, therefore honour the mysteries of Christ as friends of Christ; for otherwise he neither admits those who run to meet him, nor endures to see them with his eyes: but from his celebration 3295he bids them be exiles, and sets them down as profane and without share in the meeting with him. Let there depart with these the Eunomii and Eudoxii and Asterii, and with them the Manichaeans and the Apollinarii, the Severi and the Dioscori, and all that wretched and truly headless gangrene of the Acephali. The former, because, on account of the visible flesh of Christ, they tried to diminish and abolish his invisible and perfect Godhead too, and to make it of another nature, and to contend that it is different in essence from the nature which is in the Father who begot, boldly numbering it among created things, and decreeing that Christ God is created, and is to be adored according to both natures, the divine, I say, and the human. The latter, on account of that Godhead of Christ which is not seen, wish to make a mockery of his flesh too, and to falsify it: who, snatching away the things proper to one nature only, imagine a confusion of the conjoined essences, and gladly accept the wretched juggleries of Apollinarius and Polemon. With whom let the Ebionites and the Samosatenes, Paul's and Bardesanes', be placed, and be driven from this our feast. These, because they thought Christ only a bare man and like us, and lacking the things proper to the Godhead, and did not even name him God in any way. Those, because they affirmed that Christ was not eternal God and equal to the Father in eternity, but, as they supposed, that he became God from Mary only, from the ineffable incarnation itself.

12.

Et si quidem universi, quotquot vel impie secundum ipsos insanierunt, vel secundum eosdem evaserunt insane impii, a divino Christi occursu repellantur, ut nos, qui recte sentimus, cuncti hunc festo colentes die, hæreseos multifariæ nulla labe suscepta Christo Deo eamus obviam, eumque 3296cum sene Simeone et cum anu ac vate Anna excipiamus. Ambo enim antiquabantur, et lex videlicet, et secuti illam prophetæ, dimissionem exspectabant.
