O sanctissime Michael, qui populum Israeliticum Dei mandato per mare trajectum, in terra promissionis constituisti, et Jesum Nave contra adversarios armasti, et Moysi tabernaculum a sanguinario hoste liberasti, et multorum aliorum sanctorum animas illuminasti, meos quoque internos sensus illustra, internumque animæ meæ oculum illumina ; et cor meum, vitæ hujus fluctibus agitatum, confirma; et mentem meam ad terrena inclinatam, sursum ad coelestem prudentiam subleva; et remissas manus meas, contra bello assuetos hostes confirma; et emortuos pedes meos, ne semitam, quæ ad coelos ducit, deserant, corrobora ; et cervicem meam peccatis onustam subrige; et cicatrices meas, quæ graveolentiam exhalant, planeque computrescunt, sana; et ardentium libidinum perturbationumque incendium seda ; et ceteras tandem miseriarum et laborum meorum molestias cura. Iterum, re et nomine Michael, te oro, et quanto possum sensu obtestor, ut e vitæ hujus curriculo exituro, lætus pacatusque appareas, meque sub honorato alarum tuarum velamine abscondas atque ex angustis obscurisque inferorum locis ereptum, in loca tabernaculi admirabilis constituas, deducens « usque ad domum. Dei, in voce exsultationis, et confessionis et soni festum celebrantium Psal. xlvi, 2. . »

Tu quoque, divinissime angelorumque præstantissime Gabriel, qui mundi es exoptatissimus, gaudiique et lætitiæ dator optimus; qui divinorum arcanorum interpres es scientissimus, bonorumque omnium administer et procurator primarius ; qui sacrorum tandem ministrorum dux et princeps es augustissimus; agedum mentem et cor meum instrue, et ad universa Dei judicia et justificationes dirige. Qui Ezechieli Ezech. i, 1 seqq. in Chobar visionem illam plane divinam patefecisti, sterilitatis meæ labem dissolve.

When, then, all who are still among the living 3318die in a moment, and as it were in the twinkling of an eye, and are at once changed; when the sea and the rivers and hell, shaken with great dread and fear of the evils impending, give up the dead whom they held; when the just, borne up on the clouds of the air, are caught up to meet Christ the Lord, to live with him for all eternity, but the sinners are sent away to the punishments that are appointed for them; when at last all those wondrous things that are awaited are consummated through the sound which thou art to give: then, O holy arch-satrap, guide of the erring, raiser of the fallen, champion of souls, preserver of bodies, exterminator of demons, and illuminator of the whole creation; then, I beseech thee, then be not loath to stand by me as the faithful patron of my cause.

O most holy Michael, who by the command of God didst bring the Israelite people across through the sea and establish them in the land of promise, and didst arm Jesus Nave against his adversaries, and didst deliver the tabernacle of Moses from the bloodthirsty enemy, and didst illumine the souls of many other saints: enlighten my inward senses too, and illumine the inner eye of my soul; and strengthen my heart, tossed by the waves of this life; and lift up my mind, bent towards earthly things, to heavenly wisdom; and strengthen my slackened hands against enemies practised in war; and make firm my deadened feet, lest they forsake the path that leads to heaven; and raise up my neck, laden with sins; and heal my scars, which breathe out a foul smell and are wholly rotting; and quench the fire of burning lusts and passions; and, last, cure the other troubles of my miseries and labours. Again, Michael in deed and in name, I pray thee, and adjure thee with all the feeling I can, that when I am about to go out of the course of this life thou appear glad and at peace, and hide me under the honoured covering of thy wings, and, snatching me from the narrow and dark places of the lower world, set me in the places of the wonderful tabernacle, leading me «even to the house of God, with the voice of exultation, and of confession, and of the sound of those keeping festival.»

Thou also, most divine Gabriel, most excellent of the angels, who art the most longed-for of the world, and the best giver of gladness and joy; who art the most knowing interpreter of the divine secrets, and the chief minister and steward of all good things; who, finally, art the most august leader and prince of the sacred ministers: come, instruct my mind and my heart, and direct them to all the judgments and justifications of God. Thou who didst open to Ezekiel at Chobar that wholly divine vision, dissolve the stain of my barrenness.

Qui infecundum ante Elisabethis uterum a vinculo, quo tenebatur, Dei jussione solvisti Luc. i, 36. , eamdemque fructuosam effecisti, 3319meam quoque infelicem afflictamque animam, grata quadam venustate, internaque lætitia, et luce perfunde, qui beatissimam inculpatissimamque sacratissimæ purissimæque Dominæ nostræ Genitricis Dei Mariæ animam, læta salutarique annuntiatione ingenti gaudio imbuisti Luc. i, 28. ; Evæque tristitiam, ejusdemque luctum, certæ salutis sponsione in lætitiam transformasti ; paucis, qui totius mundi mœrorem in gaudium convertisti, vesteque luctuosa discissa, omnes lætitiæ stola induisti; te etiam atque etiam, o Gabriel, veræ lætitiæ conciliator et dator, rogo obtestorque, ut me, gravi ægritudine pressum, clementer visites, divinaque virtute atque animi jucunditate impleas, necnon a tormentis Belial, aspectabiliumque et inaspectabilium hostium periculis exemptum, per tuam auctoritatem et apud Deum intercessionem, justorum et sanctorum cœtui adjungas, et tandem in locis illis, quæ omnibus deliciis affluunt, tuto colloces.

O vos, reliqui omnes angeli et archangeli, cœtus cœlestis, corona impenetrabilis, phalanx præstantissima, multitudo immensa, exercitus innumerus, celsitudo inaccessa, magnitudo incomprehensa, chorus incomparabilis, notitia omni indagatione superior, lætitia inexplicabilis, pompa summe admirabilis, quantitas cogitationem omnem excedens, subtilitas mensuram ignorans, agilitas cui alia nulla est similis, ordo infinite multiplex, celeritas infanda, substantia quæ circumscribi ne- ç quit, gloria mentis cogitatione comprehendi nescia, vis omni virtute et robore major ; vos omnium Regis estis administri, vos venti, et spiritus, et aquæ, et Principes, et equi, et exercitus, et diaconi, et apostoli, et prædicatores, et prophetæ, et evangelistæ, et rerum divinarum interpretes, et custodes, et præsides, et conservatores, et abactores, et conductores et via duces, et inquisitores, et libripendes, et calculatores, et curatores, et illuminatores, et opum datores, et luminaria, et lucerna, et lampades, et montes, et colles, et nubes, et judices, et susceptores, et relatores, et defensores, et decertatores, et propugnatores, et divina specula, et simulacra, et imagines, et nautæ, et naves, et gubernatores.

Thou who by the bidding of God didst loose the womb of Elisabeth, before unfruitful, from the bond by which it was held, and didst make it fruitful, Ezech. i, 1 seqq. 3319flood my unhappy and afflicted soul too with a certain grateful loveliness, and with inward joy and light; thou who didst imbue with great joy, by a glad and saving annunciation, the most blessed and most blameless soul of our most sacred and most pure Lady, the Theotokos Mary, Luc. i, 36. and didst transform the sadness of Eve, and her mourning, into gladness by the pledge of sure salvation; in a word, thou who didst turn the grief of the whole world into joy, and, the garment of mourning torn apart, didst clothe all with the robe of gladness: thee again and again, O Gabriel, reconciler and giver of true gladness, I ask and adjure, that thou mercifully visit me, weighed down by grievous sickness, and fill me with divine power and joyfulness of soul; and also that, delivering me from the torments of Belial and from the perils of visible and invisible enemies, thou join me, through thy authority and intercession with God, to the company of the just and of the saints, and at last set me safely in those places which abound in all delights.

O you, all the rest of the angels and archangels, heavenly company, impenetrable crown, most excellent phalanx, immense multitude, numberless army, inaccessible loftiness, incomprehensible greatness, incomparable choir, knowledge beyond all searching, inexplicable joy, pomp supremely wonderful, quantity exceeding every thought, subtlety that knows no measure, agility to which no other is like, order infinitely manifold, unspeakable swiftness, essence that cannot be circumscribed, glory that knows not how to be grasped by the thought of the mind, force greater than every might and strength: you are the ministers of the King of all; you are winds, and spirits, and waters, and Princes, and horses, and armies, and deacons, and apostles, and preachers, and prophets, and evangelists, and interpreters of divine things, and guardians, and presidents, and preservers, and drivers-off, and conductors, and guides on the way, and inquirers, and weighers, and reckoners, and curators, and illuminators, and givers of riches, and luminaries, and lamps, and torches, and mountains, and hills, and clouds, and judges, and receivers, and reporters, and defenders, and contenders, and champions, and divine mirrors, and likenesses, and images, and sailors, and ships, and pilots.

Vos ii estis, qui coelum cursu expeditissimo transmittitis ; qui motu celerrimo incitatissimoque per aerem discurritis; qui cœlum et æthera repletis ; qui nulli non homini custodes et comites adestis; qui Creatoris voluntatem indeflexe perficitis, qui vocem sermonum ejus citra intermissionem auditis, qui semper crescitis et proficitis, perpetuaque incrementa sumitis (solus enim Deus est, in quem nulla cadit mutatio) ; qui cœlestia corpora vocamini ; qui mentes Deique tabernacula appellamini ; qui carbones, et turbines, et flammæ nominamini: qui mystæ , mysteriorumque interpretes, et omnium Regis amici, et musici, necnon sacrorum ministri, et diaconi dicimini.

3320Cum igitur tali tantaque gloria sitis exornati, totque prerogativis cumulati, merito vestram benignitatem rogo et obsecro, o boni omnisque maculæ exsortes angeli et archangeli : vestræ, o impolluti spiritus, magnificentiæ supplico, conservare vitam meam inoffensam, spem infractam, mores inculpatos, dilectionem erga Deum et proximum integram, et ab omni offensione liberam. Manu me, quæso, ducite et deducite, et ad omnem salutarem Deoque acceptam semitam dirigite. Iterum, o divini spiritus, vos rogatos plurimumque obtestatos cupio, quando in ultimo vitæ meæ hiatu, ex luteo hoc vase excedere compellar, vosque omnes actiones meas trutina expensuri estis, cunctaque, quæ vel palam, vel clam designavi, calculaturi, ut tunc vel omnes, vel ii certe, quos Deus ex vestro ordine tum mittet, quo infelicem miseramque animam meam excipiant, placidos clementesque vos mihi exhibeatis, bilancemque, quam pravis meis operibus perdite gravavi, dextere sublevetis, eamdemque tanquam solius clementis Dei ministri, quavis ratione et arte exonerare studeatis. Porro autem bonorum honestorumque operum loco, de quibus spem concipere licebat, immensam Domini misericordiam, in medium proponite, beneficum clementemque Deum, o laudatissime præstantissimæque mentes, orantes, ut per vestram intercessionem, omnes afflictiones, et morbos, et calamitates, quæ unquam in vita pertuli, in criminum meorum compensationem condemnationisque, et cruciatuum non vulgarium, quos juste promeritus fueram, relaxationem et condonationem admittere, eorumdemque vice et loco habere et calculare dignetur : quo, inimico per divinam gratiam perfecte semel superato debellatoque, ob servatam miseram animam meam, ingens gaudium oriatur in cœlo : conditore ter benignissimo vos convocante, et dicente : « Congratulamini mihi, amici, quia inveni ovem Luc. xv, 6. , » qua olim in montibus aberraverat.

You are they who cross heaven in most rapid course; who run through the air with most swift and most impetuous motion; who fill heaven and the ether; who stand by every man, none excepted, as guardians and companions; who accomplish the will of the Creator unswervingly; who hear the voice of his words without intermission; who ever grow and advance, and take continual increase (for God alone is he upon whom no change falls); who are called heavenly bodies; who are named minds and tabernacles of God; who are called coals, and whirlwinds, and flames; who are styled initiates, and interpreters of mysteries, and friends of the King of all, and musicians, and also ministers of sacred things, and deacons. 3320Since, then, you are adorned with such and so great glory, and loaded with so many prerogatives, rightly do I ask and beseech your kindness, O good angels and archangels, free of every stain: to your magnificence, O undefiled spirits, I make supplication, to preserve my life without offence, my hope unbroken, my ways blameless, my love towards God and my neighbour entire and free from every offence. Lead me, I pray, by the hand, and lead me on, and direct me to every saving path acceptable to God.

Again, O divine spirits, I would have you asked and adjured again and again, that when, at the last gasp of my life, I am compelled to go out of this vessel of clay, and you are to weigh all my actions in the balance, and to reckon up all that I have done, whether openly or in secret, then either all of you, or at least those whom God shall then send from your order to receive my unhappy and wretched soul, may show yourselves gentle and merciful to me, and may deftly lift up the scale which I have weighed down to my ruin with my evil works, and, as ministers of the only merciful God, may be zealous to unburden it by every means and art. And furthermore, in place of good and honourable works, of which I might have conceived hope, set forth in the midst the immense mercy of the Lord, praying the beneficent and merciful God, O most praised and most excellent minds, that through your intercession he may deign to admit all the afflictions, and diseases, and calamities which I have ever borne in life, in compensation for my crimes, and for the remission and pardon of the condemnation and of the uncommon torments which I had justly deserved, and to hold and reckon them in their stead and place: so that, the enemy having once been perfectly overcome and vanquished by divine grace, great joy may arise in heaven over my wretched soul, saved; the thrice most kind Maker calling you together, and saying: «Rejoice with me, friends, for I have found the sheep» Luc. xv, 6. that once had strayed in the mountains.

Denique, cum consummationis dies advenerit, et mundi finis jam pre foribus urserit, et coeli contremiscent, et firmamentum nutabit, et terræ fundamenta quatientur, et abyssus abyssi præ timore obrigescet, et inferi formidine horrebunt, et immensi maris divisiones in ignem vertentur, et elementa omnia a facie ignis, ut cera, dissolventur, et qui in terre recessibus obruti jacebunt, exsuscitabuntur, et mundi hujus figura pertransibit, et tremendus judex in limine ipso assistet, vosque adventum illius antevertentes, terminos finesque terræ percurretis, quo universos mortales, qui a sæculo mortem obierant, in unum cogatis, illosque incorrupto judici simul omnes sistatis, quo singuli de omni opere, omnique cogitatione, et animi conceptione rationem reddant : tunc, obsecro vos, neque unquam usque ad vitæ finem obsecrare desinam, qui omnia lustratis, publicaque Dei ministeria non absque auctoritate obitis, et hoc ipso 3321regiam quamdam dignitatem præ vobis geritis, quod et sceptra, et virgas, et hastas defertis (per hæc enim coercendi, et puniendi vim, qua polletis, evidenter ostenditis), tunc, inquam, tunc, obsecro, propitii adeste, miseroque, et infelici tanquam ter optimi regis illius principes, parcite, et me a tristi — illa, iracundaque sententia, qua in ignem æternum amandat, per vestram apud Deum intercessionem liberate. Contra vero lætæ, et jucunda illius vocationis, vitæque, quæ omni maerore caret, nullaque re indiget, me una cum meis synergis, et cognatis, quorum nomina, et vocationem novit is, qui nihil nescit, participem efficite, et gaudii tandem quod nunquam finem facit, haeredem declarate.

O Domine, Servator, Deus et Verbum, qui dominatum in excelso obtines : qui principio cares, universaque contines : qui ingenti robore polles, omnibus vitam, omnibus timorem affers; qui cælum et terram, omniaque, quæ illorum ambitu continentur, solo nutu condidisti Esther. xiii, 10. ; qui primum quidem angelos mente concepisti, tuusque con- — ceptus opus fuit : quod enim mente conceperas, id solo verbo explesti, et absolvisti. Mox pari arte et facilitate hominem e terre pulvere, secundum tuam imaginem et similitudinem formasti Gen. i, 26, 27. , effor- — matumque, et modicum quid ab angelis diminutum Psal. viii, 6. , et libero arbitrio ornatum, omnium crea- — tarum rerum regem et dominum constituisti; nempe quo bonis illis, quæ illi in paradiso præparaveras, frueretur, quibus etiam tantisper fruitus est dum a serpente, et uxore captus, atque seductus, divina tua mandata prævaricatus est, degustando nimirum lethiferæ arboris pomum : quare e regia dignitate e vestigio excidit, paradisoque exclusus, in poenam factis suis dignam incurrit; — puta in fletum, et servilem habitum, in sudorem, — laborem et molestiam, in tristitiam et dolorem, et quod suppliciorum omnium vere est acerbissimum, in justam tandem mortem, dissolutionemque in terram, ex qua primo susceptus, productusque fuerat.

Finally, when the day of the consummation has come, and the end of the world now presses at the doors, and the heavens tremble, and the firmament totters, and the foundations of the earth are shaken, and the abyss of the abyss stiffens for fear, and the lower world shudders with dread, and the divisions of the immense sea are turned into fire, and all the elements melt like wax before the face of the fire, and those who lie buried in the recesses of the earth are raised up, and the fashion of this world passes away, and the dreadful Judge stands at the very threshold; and you, going before his coming, run through the bounds and ends of the earth, to gather into one all mortals who from the beginning of the world had met death, and to set them all together before the incorrupt Judge, so that each may render account of every work, and of every thought, and of the conceiving of his mind: then, I beseech you (and never, up to the end of my life, shall I cease to beseech you), you who survey all things, and discharge the public ministries of God not without authority, and by this very fact 3321bear before you a certain royal dignity, that you carry sceptres, and rods, and spears (for by these you plainly show the power of coercing and of punishing in which you are strong): then, I say, then, I beseech you, be present and propitious, and, as princes of that thrice-best King, spare one wretched and unhappy, and through your intercession with God deliver me from that sad and wrathful sentence by which it sends men away into the eternal fire. But on the contrary make me, together with my fellow-workers and kinsmen, whose names and calling he knows who is ignorant of nothing, a partaker of that glad and pleasant calling, and of the life that lacks all grief and wants for nothing, and declare me heir, at last, of the joy that never makes an end.

O Lord, Saviour, God and Word, who holdest dominion on high: who lackest beginning, and containest all things: who art strong in great might, and bringest life to all, fear to all; who didst found heaven and earth, and all that is contained in their compass, by thy nod alone; who first conceived the angels in thy mind, and thy conception was a work: for what thou hadst conceived in mind, that thou didst fulfil and complete by a word alone. Then with like art and ease thou didst form man from the dust of the earth according to thine image and likeness, and, having formed him, and made him a little less than the angels, and adorned him with free will, thou didst set him as king and lord of all created things; that is, so that he might enjoy those good things which thou hadst prepared for him in paradise, which he did indeed enjoy for a while, until, taken and seduced by the serpent and his wife, he transgressed thy divine commandments, by tasting, namely, the fruit of the death-bearing tree: wherefore he fell forthwith from his royal dignity, and, shut out of paradise, incurred a punishment worthy of his deeds, to wit, weeping and a servile condition, sweat, toil and trouble, sadness and pain, and, what is truly the bitterest of all punishments, a just death at last, and dissolution into the earth from which he had first been taken up and brought forth. Gen. i, 26, 27.

Tu autem, Domine, vere clemens, et misericors, 3322peculium, quod præ cæteris omnibus tibi charum, diaboli tyrannis oppresserat, nequaquam aspernatus es; verum tremendum illud mysterium, quod ante omnia sæcula definieras, expleturus, teipsum, qui Patris es sapientia, et mens, et Ver— bum, et character, et terminus, et imago, et sigillum, et Filius, et forma expressa, exinanivisti. Nam et in casta Virgine per Spiritum sanctum inhabitasti, et ortum ex ea, qui omnem rationem excedit, sumpsisti. Nec enim virginea claustra omnino solvisti; sed carnis expers, modo, quem tu nosti, in eam ingressus, carne indutus ex ea denuo egressus es, uteri ostio clauso, ut ante erat, relicto. Mox crucem subiisti, felle, et aceto inter se permistis, potatus, lanceaque ad latus tuum adacta, confossus. Mortem tandem sponte perpessus, mortem ipsam peremisti, mundumque universum immortalitate donasti; et prolapsam, deformatamque imaginem, in integrum instaurasti, et veterem maledictionem, que per lignum irrogata — fuerat delevisti. Tu ergo universorum Rex et Conditor, qui unus tantarum, tamque admirabilium rerum architectus es, cunctaque, que usque exstant, in communem mortalium utilitatem condi— disti, ac pœnæ obnoxios non sine paterna affectione punire consuevisti, quando pro tribunali sedens, — omnes ad judicium es adducturus, statione dextera cum iis omnibus, quorum memoriam in mente habeo, me dignare. O omnium inspector, occultorumque cognitor, criminibus, que ullo unquam tempore perpetravi, oblivioni traditis, immortalis thalami ostium, hoc est, cœlestem gloriam mihi pande. O immortalis Sponse, benedictionis vocem fac nos audire. O benefice, justorum numero me — accense, tuique gaudii, inculpatæ Matris tuæ, an— gelorumque sanctorum, et apostolorum, et prophetarum, et martyrum, et justorum omnium, qui a sæculo fuerunt, precibus intercedentibus, consortes nos effice, qui cum Patre tuo, et Spiritu sancto es benedictus nunc et semper, et in sæcula sæculorum. Amen.

But thou, Lord, truly merciful and compassionate, 3322didst by no means spurn thy own possession, dear to thee beyond all others, which the tyranny of the devil had oppressed; but, being about to fulfil that tremendous mystery which thou hadst determined before all ages, thou didst empty thyself, who art the Father's wisdom, and mind, and Word, and character, and boundary, and image, and seal, and Son, and expressed form. For thou didst dwell in the chaste Virgin through the Holy Spirit, and didst take from her a birth that exceeds all reason. For neither didst thou at all loose the virginal enclosures; but, being without flesh, having entered into her in a manner which thou knowest, clothed with flesh thou didst go out from her again, the door of the womb being left closed, as it was before. Then thou didst undergo the cross, given to drink gall and vinegar mixed together, and pierced with a lance driven into thy side. Suffering death at last of thy own will, thou didst slay death itself, and didst endow the whole world with immortality; and thou didst restore the fallen and deformed image to wholeness, and didst blot out the old curse which had been brought on through the tree. Thou therefore, King and Maker of all things, who alone art the architect of things so great and so wonderful, and didst found all things that still exist for the common profit of mortals, and art wont to punish those liable to punishment not without a father's affection: when, sitting on the tribunal, thou art to lead all to judgment, deem me worthy of a standing on the right hand, with all those whose memory I hold in mind. O overseer of all, and knower of hidden things, consigning to oblivion the crimes which I have at any time committed, open to me the door of the immortal bridal chamber, that is, heavenly glory. O immortal Bridegroom, make us hear the voice of blessing. O beneficent one, enrol me in the number of the just, and make us partakers of thy joy, by the intercessory prayers of thy blameless Mother, and of the holy angels, and of the apostles, and prophets, and martyrs, and all the just who have been from the beginning of the world; who with thy Father and the Holy Spirit art blessed now and ever, and to the ages of ages. Amen.

3321Ἐγκώμιον εἰς τὸν ἅγιον Ἰωάννην τὸν Πρόδρομον.

αʹ.

3321ENCOMIUM OF SAINT JOHN THE FORERUNNER

1.

Δίδου, ὦ φωνὴ τοῦ Λόγου, φωνήν· δίδου ἡμῖν, ὦ λύχνε τοῦ φωτὸς, τὴν αὐγήν· δίδου ἡμῖν, ὦ τοῦ Λόγου Πρόδρομε, τοῦ λόγου τὸν δρόμον, ἵνα σε πρὸς ἀξίαν τοῖς σοῖς εὐφημήσαντες ἐντρυφήσωμεν σήμερον· ὑμνεῖν γάρ σε κατὰ χρέος πατρῷόν τε καὶ ἰδικῶν πάντων πλέον, ὦ Βαπτιστὰ, προθυμούμεθα. 3324Ἐπειδὴ δὲ πάντων πλέον ἐκ προγόνων σου ταῖς δωρεαῖς ἐνεβρύνθημεν, καὶ οἴκοθεν τοῦτο δρᾷν οὐ δυνάμενοι, πάλιν ἐπὶ σὲ τὸν φιλόδωρον τρέχομεν, καὶ σὲ γενέσθαι χορηγὸν ἡμῖν τῶν ἐπαίνων αἰτούμεθα. Ἵνα μή σε μόνον τοῖς ἐξ ἡμῶν λόγοις γεραίροντες, ὑβρισταί τινες εἶναι τῆς μικροπρεπείας τῶν λόγων καὶ ἀτιμίας δόξωμεν, ἢ ἐπαινέται τε καὶ εὐχάριστοι, παρὰ τοῖς οὐκ εἰδόσι τὴν ἡμῶν ἐν λόγοις εὐτέλειαν, ἀλλὰ τῷ μεγέθει ἢ ἀξίᾳ τοῦ τιμωμένου ἀπαιτοῦσιν εἶναι τὸν λόγον ἐφάμιλλον· καὶ μὴ διδοῦσι συγγνώμην τῇ προθέσει τοῦ λέγοντος, τοῦ πόθου τὴν φλόγα μὴ φερούσῃ, εἰ τὸ ὑπὲρ αὐτὴν ἐκ βίας ἐφάπτοιτο. Ὅτι μὲν γὰρ οἵ σε ταῖς εὐφημίαις στεφανοῦν ἐθελήσοιεν πόῤῥω που τῆς ἀξίας καὶ πάνυ μακρὸν ἀπολείπονται, οὐκέτι οὐδεὶς ὡς οὐχ ὅλῳ κηρύξειε τῷ στόματι, εἰ μὴ μέμηνε καὶ τοῦ κατὰ φύσιν ἐξέστη φρονήματος. Τί οὖν χρὴ διαπράττεσθαι τὸν οἷον ἐμὲ μυρίοις σου καθ᾿ ὥραν ἑκάστην δωρεαῖς, ὥσπερ τι δένδρον παρ᾿ ὕδασι τὴν φυτείαν λαχὸν, ἀρδευόμενον καὶ ἀλήκτως παραῤῥέουσαν ἔχοντα τῆς σῆς ἐνεργητικῆς χύσεως τὴν ἐπίδοσιν; Ἄρα σιγᾶν καὶ μηδὲν ὅλως βοᾷν χαριστήριον ὅτι κατ᾿ ἀξίαν μὴ δεδύνηται, ἢ βοᾷν ὡς οἷός τε εἴη καὶ δύνηται, καὶ ταύτῃ δεικνύναι τῆς ἐνούσης αὐτῷ γνώμης τὸ ἀσχετον; κἂν ἡ βοὴ ταύτης τὸ μέγεθος δι᾿ οἰκείαν φανεροῦν μὴ ἰσχύσῃ σμικρότητα. Ὁ μὲν γὰρ καὶ ἀποδοχῆς ἄξιος κρίνεται, κἂν τὴν τῶν εὐγνωμονούντων μοῖραν λογίζηται· καθὰ κἀκεῖνος ὁ ἐν τῇ δεκᾶδι τῶν ὑπὸ τοῦ Σωτῆρος καθαρθέντων λεπρῶν ἀλλογενὴς καὶ ἀλλόφυλος· ὁ δὲ τῷ τῶν ἀχαρίστων στίφει συνάπτεται, τὸν εὐεργέτην οὐκ εὐφημῶν κατὰ δύναμιν, καθὰ καὶ οὗτοι εἰληφότες μὲν τῆς νόσου τὴν κάθαρσιν, οὐ δεδωκότες δὲ δόξαν Χριστῷ τῷ ταύτην αὐτοῖς δεδωκότι τὴν κάθαρσιν.

βʹ. Οὐκοῦν οὐ σιωπητέον, λεκτέον δέ σοι ποθητῶς τὸ ἐγκώμιον, εἰ καὶ κατόπιν τῆς σῆς μεγαλειότητος λέξοιμεν· ὡς ἂν μὴ τούτων τῶν ἐννέα τὸν μῶμον δρεψόμεθα ἀλλὰ τοῦ κριθέντος εὐγνώμονος τὴν ἐπαινετὴν ἑταιρίαν ἁρπάσωμεν. Βόησον τοίνυν, ὦ Βαπτιστὰ, καὶ νῦν ἐν ἡμῖν ὡς ἐν ἐρήμῳ τὸ πρότερον. Ἔρημοι γὰρ καὶ ἡμεῖς λόγου καὶ φωνῆς καὶ ταῦτα βλαστώσης αὐγῆς καθεστήκαμεν· ἐξ ὧν σοι καὶ δι᾿ ὧν ὁ τῆς εὐφημίας ἀναπλέκεται στέφανος· βόησον καὶ νῦν ἐν ἡμῖν γεγωνότερον. Βοησόμεθα γὰρ εἰ βοήσειας, καὶ σιγήσομεν εἰ σιγήσειας, κἂν μεγαλοφώνως βοᾷν προῃρήμεθα. Εἰς οὐδὲν γὰρ ἡμῶν ἡ βοὴ λογισθήσεται, μὴ τῇ θειοτέρᾳ σου βοῇ τὸ σθένος λαμβάνουσα· καὶ οὐδαμόθι γῆς ἡμῶν ἡ φωνὴ ἀκουσθήσεται, μὴ ἐκ τῆς μεγάλης σου φωνῆς δεχόμενοι τὸ εὔηχον. Διὸ μᾶλλον καὶ μᾶλλον εἰς συμμαχίαν σε προκαλούμεθα καὶ λῦσαι δεόμεθα τὴν γλῶτταν ἡμῶν ἀφωνίᾳ δουλεύουσαν, ὥσπερ καὶ Ζαχαρίας τῷ πατρὶ πάλαι γενόμενος ἔλυσας· καὶ δοῦναι φωνὴν ἱκετεύομεν πρὸς τὴν τῆς σῆς εὐφημίας ἀνάῤῥησιν, καθὰ κἀκείνῳ τικτόμενος δέδωκας πρὸς τὴν τοῦ σοῦ δημηγορίαν ὀνόματος.

Give, O voice of the Word, a voice; give us, O lamp of the light, the radiance; give us, O Forerunner of the Word, the running of the word, that, praising thee as befits thy deeds, we may take our delight today: for we are eager to hymn thee, O Baptist, both by an ancestral debt and more than all that are our own. 3324But since, more than all, we have taken pride in thy gifts from thy forebears' time, and are not able to do this from our own store, we run again to thee, the lover of giving, and ask thee to become the provider of our praises; lest, honouring thee only with words of our own, we should seem to be insulters of thee through the meanness and dishonour of our words, rather than praisers and thankful, among those who do not know our cheapness in words, but demand that the speech be a match for the greatness or worth of the one honoured, and grant no pardon to the purpose of the speaker, which does not bear the flame of its longing, if it should lay hold by force on what is beyond it. For that those who would wish to crown thee with praises fall far short of thy worth and are left very far behind, there is no longer anyone who would not proclaim it with his whole mouth, unless he is mad and has gone out of his natural mind. What then must one like me do, who every hour am watered with thy countless gifts, like some tree that has had its planting by the waters, and have the bounty of thy operative outpouring flowing past me without ceasing? Is it to keep silent and cry out no thanksgiving at all, because he has not been able to do it worthily; or to cry out as far as he is able and can, and in this way show how irrepressible is the disposition within him, even if the cry is not strong enough, through its own smallness, to make its greatness manifest? For the one is judged worthy of acceptance, and would be reckoned in the portion of the grateful, as was that one among the ten lepers cleansed by the Saviour, the foreigner and of another race; but the other is joined to the band of the ungrateful, not praising his benefactor according to his power, as these too, who received the cleansing of the disease but did not give glory to Christ who had given them this cleansing.

2. So then we must not keep silent, but thine encomium must be spoken by us with longing, even if we speak it far behind thy greatness; so that we may not reap the reproach of those nine, but may seize the praiseworthy fellowship of him who was judged grateful. Cry out then, O Baptist, now too in us, as in the desert before. For we too have become a desert of word and of voice, and of the radiance that makes these sprout; out of which and through which the crown of praise is plaited for thee. Cry out now too in us more loudly. For we shall cry out if thou shouldst cry out, and we shall be silent if thou shouldst be silent, even if we had chosen to cry out with a great voice. For our cry will be reckoned as nothing, if it does not take its strength from thy more divine cry; and nowhere on earth will our voice be heard, unless we receive its fine sound from thy great voice. Wherefore more and more we call thee to our alliance, and beg thee to loose our tongue that is enslaved to voicelessness, as thou didst loose it long ago for Zacharias thy father when thou wast born; and we beseech thee to give a voice for the proclaiming of thy praise, as at thy birth thou gavest it to him for the public declaring of thy name.

Εἰ γὰρ ἐκεῖνος τὴν γλῶτταν πεπέδητο ἕως αὐτὴν ἡ σὴ προέλευσις ἕλυσε, καὶ τῆς σῆς προσηγορίας κεκίνηκε πρόοδος, πῶς ἂν ἡμεῖς πρὸς τὴν σὴν εὐφημίαν τὴν γλῶτταν 3325κινήσοιμεν, μὴ σοῦ ταύτην κινοῦντος καὶ στρέφοντος καὶ πρὸς τὸ σὸν αὐτὴν κατευθύνοντος στάδιον;

γʹ. Ἀλλά μοι πρὸς τὴν βαλβίδα τῶν λόγων ὁ λόγος ἐλθὼν, καὶ πρὸς τὴν τῶν σῶν θαυμάτων πληθὺν ἐκθαμβούμενος, ἀπορίᾳ συνέχεται μείζονι, ὅθεν τῆς κατὰ σὲ διηγήσεως ἄρξηται. Τὴν γέννησιν εἴποι πρώτην ἣν ὁ Γαβριὴλ προεμήνυσε ὡς χαρᾶς ἐσομένην τῷ κόσμῳ προοίμιον; Ἀλλὰ τὴν κυοφορίαν ἣν ἡ στεῖρα γραῦς ὡς ὑπερφυᾶ περιέκρυβε; Ἀλλὰ τὴν ἐγγάστριον ὄρχησιν τὴν πρώτην Θεὸν ἐγγάστριον πᾶσι κηρύξασαν; Ἀλλὰ τὴν ἐκ στείρας ἀπότεξιν ἱστορεῖν ἐπεθύμησε; Ἀλλὰ καὶ πορείαν τριήμερον ἤνυσε καὶ χρόνον τριμηνιαῖον παρέμεινεν, ἵνα τὴν θαυμαστήν σου θεωρήσασα γέννησιν τὸ τῆς Τριάδος ἑνὸς μήτηρ ἀπειρόγαμος ἔσεσθαι οὐ συνελήφθη βεβαίως τε καὶ ἀραρότως πιστεύσειεν; Ἀλλὰ τὴν γαλούχησιν ἣν ξηρός σοι μασθὸς θηλὴν διὰ γῆρας βαθὺ παραδόξως προέχευεν; Ἀλλὰ τὴν ἐν ἐρήμῳ δίαιταν ἣν ὁ κόσμος χωρεῖν οὐκ ἠδύνατο, τὴν ἐκ τῆς ἐρήμου πρὸς τὸν Ἰσραὴλ τελευταίαν καὶ πρώτην ἀνάδειξιν δι᾿ ἧς αὐτοῖς τὸν Χριστὸν ἐξεκάλυπτε, καὶ τὴν τῶν οὐρανῶν βασιλείαν ἐκήρυττε. Ἀλλὰ τὸν Ἰορδάνην ἐν ᾧ τὸν λεὼν ἐκαθάριζες, καὶ δι᾿ οὗ σε Χριστὸς ἐκαθάρισε, λειτουργόν σε λαβὼν ποιήσας τοῦ οἰκείου βαπτίσματος; Τὴν νομοθεσίαν ἣν φιλανθρώπως τοῖς πειθομένοις ἐθέσπιζες; Τὴν ἀπειλὴν ἣν ἐμβριθῶς τοῖς ἀπειθοῦσι προέφερες; Τὴν παῤῥησίαν δι᾿ ἧς ἄρχοντας καὶ βασιλέας κατέπληττες, καὶ ἣν ἀξίως ὁ βασιλεὺς Ἡρώδης ἐτρόμασε, καὶ εἰ πάθει γυναίου τὸ δέος ἀπέβαλε; Τὴν γενναίαν ἀποτομὴν καὶ τῆς τιμίας κεφαλῆς τὴν ἀφαίρεσιν, δι᾿ ἧς σεαυτὸν διπλοῦν τοῦ Σωτῆρος κατέστησας Πρόδρομον, οὐκ ἐπὶ γῆς μόνον αὐτοῦ προϊὼν καὶ προτρέχων, ἀλλὰ καὶ εἰς ᾅδην γιγνόμενος Πρόδρομος καραδοκοῦσι καὶ μένουσιν, ὥσπερ τοῖς ἐπὶ γῆς προκηρύττων αὐτοῦ τὴν σωτήριον ἄφεσιν;

δʹ. Ἀλλὰ τί τῶν σῶν ἐπέλθωμεν πρότερον· ἢ τί καταλείψωμεν δεύτερον; ἑκάστου τὴν ὑπεροχὴν σύγκληρον ἔχοντος καὶ φιλονεικοῦντος εἰκότως εἰς προοίμιον τάττεσθαι καὶ τὸ πέρας ἐφ᾿ ἑαυτῷ πάσης τῆς παρούσης ἐγκωμιαστικῆς διαλέξεως δέχεσθαι, καὶ μὴ συγχωροῦντος ἐπ᾿ ἄλλο τι τὴν γλῶτταν διήγημα φέρεσθαι, ὅπως ἂν ἀτελὲς αὐτῷ μὴ ἐαθῇ καὶ ἀποπλήρωτον. Δοκεῖ δὲ ἡμῖν ἔχειν καλῶς εἰ τοῦ χρόνου καὶ ἡμεῖς τὴν τάξιν φυλάξοιμεν, καὶ τῷ χρόνῳ πρωτεύουσαν, καὶ νῦν πρωτεύσοι ταῖς λέξεσιν· ἵνα καὶ τάξιν λάβοι τὴν πρέπουσαν, ἁρμόδιον ἑκάστῳ καὶ βραχεῖαν παρέχων διήγησιν, καὶ φύγοι τῆς χρονικῆς ἐναλλαγῆς τὴν ἀνάχυσιν· ἐπαινῶν ἐν τούτῳ Λάβαν τὸν δόλιον, ὅτι τῶν θυγατέρων τὴν ἥττονα πρὸ τῆς πρεσβυτέρας γάμῳ κατεγγυᾷν οὐκ ἠνέσχετο, καὶ ταῦτα ποθουμένην καὶ πρόκριτον νομισθεῖσαν τῷ γήμαντι· οὐ μόνης χάριν σωματικῆς, ἀλλὰ καὶ ψυχικῆς ὡραιότητος ἕνεκα;

For if he was fettered in his tongue until thy coming forth loosed it, and the going forth of thy name set it moving, how should we move our tongue to thy praise, 3325unless thou move and turn it, and direct it to thy own course?

3. But my speech, having come to the starting-line of my words, and astounded at the multitude of thy wonders, is held fast by a greater perplexity, whence it should begin the narrative concerning thee. Should it tell first of the birth which Gabriel announced beforehand as to be a prelude of joy to the world? Or the pregnancy which the barren old woman hid as a thing beyond nature? Or the leaping in the womb, the first, which proclaimed to all God in the womb? Or did it desire to recount the bearing from a barren woman? But she also accomplished a three days' journey, and remained for a space of three months, that, having beheld thy wonderful birth, she might believe firmly and surely that she, the unwedded one, would be mother of One of the Trinity — was not conceived [?] —? Or the suckling, which a breast dry with deep old age paradoxically poured forth for thee from its nipple? Or the way of life in the desert, which the world could not contain; the showing from the desert to Israel, last and first, through which he revealed the Christ to them, and preached the kingdom of heaven? Or the Jordan, in which thou didst cleanse the people, and through which Christ cleansed thee, taking thee and making thee the minister of his own baptism? The lawgiving which thou didst ordain with love for man for those who obeyed? The threat which thou didst weightily set before the disobedient? The boldness by which thou didst strike rulers and kings with dismay, and which king Herod rightly dreaded, even if through passion for a mere woman he cast off his fear? The noble beheading, and the taking off of the precious head, by which thou madest thyself the twofold Forerunner of the Saviour, not only going forth and running before him on earth, but also becoming Forerunner in Hades to those who were waiting and remaining there, as to those on earth, proclaiming beforehand his saving remission?

4. But which of thy deeds shall we come to first, or which shall we leave for second? Each has pre-eminence as its share, and contends with reason to be set in the prelude and to receive in itself the end of the whole present encomiastic discourse, and does not allow the tongue to be carried off to some other narrative, lest it be left by it incomplete and unfilled. But it seems good to us if we too should keep the order of time, and what is first in time should now be first in our words also; that it may receive its fitting order, offering a fitting and brief narrative to each, and may escape the confusion of chronological interchange: praising in this Laban the crafty, because he did not endure to betroth in marriage the lesser of his daughters before the elder, and that though she was desired and preferred by him who married her, not for the sake of bodily beauty alone, but of the soul's beauty too?

Οὐκοῦν ἐπαναγέσθω πάλιν ἡμῖν ὁ λόγος ἐπὶ τὴν ἀρχὴν τοῦ λόγου καὶ τοῦ θαυμαζομένου τὴν σύλληψιν, εἰ τῆς εἰς τὸ εἶναι παρόδου προοίμιον καὶ πάντων ἐφεξῆς ἐν τάξει τιθέτω τὸν ἔπαινον, ὡς αὐτὸς ὁ παρ᾿ ἡμῶν εὐφημούμενος τὸν λόγον ἡμῖν χορηγεῖν ἐπινεύσειεν. 3328Τάχα δὲ καὶ ταύτης ἀνωτέρω τὸν λόγον ἀγαγεῖν οὐκ ἀσύμφορον· δείξειε γὰρ καὶ οὕτως αὐτὸν καὶ πρὸ ταύτης τῆς ἐν κοιλίᾳ συλλήψεως μέγαν τινὰ γενησόμενον καὶ ἐπὶ μειζόνων ἀγαθῶν προσδοκίᾳ φανούμενον, τὴν ἐν Ἀδὰμ ἡμῶν οὐκ ἀγνοούμενον ἔκπτωσιν. Διὰ ταύτης γὰρ πάντως ἀθλίως ἐπράττομεν καὶ εἰς γῆν καὶ φθορὰν ἀπελήγομεν, ὡς ἅπαξ οἱ πάντες τὸ, «Γῆ εἶ, καὶ εἰς γῆν ἀπελεύσῃ,» πειθόμενοι· τοῦτο γὰρ τῆς ἡμῶν ἐτύγχανε φύσεως ὑπὲρ τῆς ἡμῶν παρακοῆς τὸ κατάκριμα. Οὐκ αὐτὴ δὲ μόνη τῆς ἀνθρωπίνης ζωῆς ἡ ἀθλιότης ἐδείκνυτο, ἀλλ᾿ εἶχεν αὐτῇ συμμεμιγμένον τὸ ἄθεον. Τὸν γὰρ Κτίστην τοῦ παντὸς καὶ Δεσπότην ἐάσαντες τοῖς ὁμοδούλοις ἐλατρεύομεν κτίσμασιν, καὶ θεοὺς ὠνομάζομεν τῶν ἰδίων ἡμῶν χειρῶν τὰ ποιήματα. Διὸ καὶ τοῖς τῆς ἀτιμίας ἐδουλεύομεν πάθεσι, ἐπειδὴ τοῖς ἀτίμοις εἰδώλοις καὶ δαίμοσιν τοῦ μόνου τιμίου Θεοῦ τὴν τιμὴν προσεφέρομεν. Τοῦτο τῆς ἡμῶν φύσεως ἦν τὸ ἀῤῥώστημα, ὃ μεγέθους ὑπερβολὴν οὐ δεχόμενον μεγίστης ἰατρείας ἐδέετο. Αὕτη δὲ ἦν τὸ τὸν Κτίστην ἡμῖν ὁμοιωθῆναι τοῖς κτίσμασιν, καὶ τὸν Θεὸν ἄνθρωπον γενέσθαι καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς διὰ τῆς πρὸς σάρκα τὴν ἀνθρωπίνην συνθέσεως· καὶ πάντων ἡμῶν ἀναλαβεῖν ἐν ἑαυτῷ τὰ φυσικὰ καὶ διαβολὴν οὐκ ἐμποιοῦντα παθήματα· καὶ τοῦτον παντελῆ τὸν ἀφορισμὸν ἐπιδείξασθαι, καὶ οὕτω περιφανῶς διασώσασθαι τὸν τούτοις ἑκουσίως κρατηθέντα τε καὶ κρατούμενον ἄνθρωπον, καὶ πρὸς τὴν πρώτην αὐτὸν ἐπανάγειν μακαριότητα, ἣν αὐτῷ καὶ ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς δημιουργήσας συνέζευξε.

εʹ. Ταύτην Θεὸς καὶ ποιεῖν πρώτῳ τῷ Ἀβραὰμ ἐπηγγείλατο, καὶ ἐν τῷ σπέρματι αὐτοῦ πάσας εὐλογηθῆναι τὰς φυλὰς τῆς γῆς ἐχαρίσατο, διὰ τῆς ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ τοῦ Μονογενοῦς αὐτοῦ Υἱοῦ καὶ Λόγου σαρκώσεως· ταύτην μετὰ τὸν Ἀβραὰμ τῷ Δαβὶδ ἐπωμόσατο, καὶ ἐκ καρποῦ τῆς κοιλίας αὐτοῦ ἐπὶ τὸν βασιλικώτατον αὐτοῦ θρόνον τὸν βασιλέα τῆς δόξης τιθέναι συνέθετο, ὅτ᾿ ἂν ὁ παρὼν αἰὼν ἐπὶ τὸ τέλος ἀφίκοιτο καὶ τοῦ γραπτοῦ νόμου ὁ διορισθεὶς ὑπ᾿ αὐτοῦ καιρὸς τὸ πλήρωμα δέξοιτο. Ἣν ἰδεῖν καὶ θεάσασθαι οὐ μόνος Ἀβραὰμ καὶ Δαβὶδ οἱ τὰς τοιαύτας ἐπαγγελίας ἀξιωθέντες κληρώσασθαι, ἀλλὰ καὶ πολλοὶ μετ᾿ ἐκείνους προφῆταί τε καὶ βασιλεῖς ἐπεθύμησαν, καθάπερ ἐν Εὐαγγελίοις πρὸς τὴν τῶν οἰκείων μαθητῶν μακαρίαν ὁμήγυριν, αὐτὸς ἡ πάντων ἁγίων ἐπιθυμία Χριστὸς ἀπεφήνατο. Παρῆν οὖν τοῦ παρόντος αἰῶνος τὰ ἔσχατα, καὶ ἡ ἐπαγγελία τὴν οἰκείαν ἑκάστου ἐνέκβασιν, καὶ ὁ νόμος τὴν προσδοκωμένην αὐτῷ διὰ Χριστοῦ προσεδέχετο πλήρωσιν· καὶ πάντες οἱ ἐν Ἰερουσαλὴμ προφῆται καὶ δίκαιοι τὴν τοῦ Ἰσραὴλ προσδεχόμενοι λύτρωσιν, καὶ Πνεύματι ταύτην ἐφεστῶσαν μανθάνοντες, ἐπόθουν αὐτῆς τὴν παρουσίαν θεάσασθαι, οὐ μόνον ψυχῆς ἀλλὰ καὶ σώματος ὄμμασιν πρὶν τῆς παρούσης ζωῆς ἐκδημήσωσιν. Καὶ οὐ μόνον ἐπόθουν, ἀλλὰ καὶ ηὔχοντο καὶ Θεῷ δεήσεις προσέφερον, καὶ τῶν αἰτουμένων (ἄξιοι γὰρ ἦσαν) ἐτύγχανον.

So then let our speech be brought back again to the beginning of the speech, and to the conception of the one admired, if [?] the prelude of his passage into being, and let it set his praise in order through all things successively, as he himself who is praised by us may 3328nod consent to furnish us the speech. And perhaps it is not unprofitable to carry the speech higher even than this; for so too it would show him, even before this conception in the womb, as one who would be great and would appear in the expectation of greater good things, in view of our fall in Adam, a fall not unknown. For through this we fared wretchedly in every way, and ended in earth and corruption, all of us having yielded once for all to the sentence, «Earth thou art, and unto earth shalt thou depart»: for this was the condemnation of our nature for our disobedience. And this wretchedness of human life was not the only thing shown, but it had godlessness mingled with it. For, leaving the Creator and Master of all, we worshipped creatures, our fellow-slaves, and named as gods the works of our own hands. Wherefore also we were enslaved to the passions of dishonour, since to dishonourable idols and demons we offered the honour of the only honourable God. This was the sickness of our nature, which, admitting no excess beyond its greatness, needed the greatest healing. And this was: that the Creator be made like to us his creatures, and God become man like us through the composition with human flesh; and take up in himself the natural sufferings of all of us, those that bring no reproach; and show forth this complete distinction, and so manifestly save the man who willingly had been and was being held fast by these, and lead him back to the first blessedness, which he had joined to him when he fashioned him from the beginning.

5. This God promised first to Abraham to do, and granted that in his seed all the tribes of the earth should be blessed, through the incarnation, from him, of his only-begotten Son and Word; this, after Abraham, he swore to David, and covenanted to set from the fruit of his womb the King of glory upon his most royal throne, when the present age should come to its end, and the time determined by him for the written law should receive its fulfilment. This not Abraham and David alone, who were deemed worthy to receive such promises as their lot, but also many prophets and kings after them desired to see and behold, as in the Gospels Christ himself, the desire of all the saints, declared to the blessed assembly of his own disciples. The last things of the present age, then, were at hand; and the promise was receiving its proper outcome for each, and the law the fulfilment expected for it through Christ; and all the prophets and righteous in Jerusalem, awaiting the redemption of Israel, and learning by the Spirit that it was at hand, longed to behold its presence, not only with the eyes of the soul but also with those of the body, before they should depart from the present life. And not only did they long, but they also prayed, and offered petitions to God, and obtained what they asked (for they were worthy).

Τοιοῦτος ἦν ὁ Σιμεὼν ὁ Χριστὸν ὡς ἱερεὺς ἐν ἀγκάλαις δεξάμενος, καὶ Δεσπότην αὐτὸν πάσης ὁμολογήσας τῆς 3329κτίσεως. Τοιαύτη ἡ Ἄννα ἡ τὴν πολυχρόνιον ἐκείνην χηρείαν κοσμίως ἀνύσασα, καὶ νύκτα καὶ ἡμέραν ἐν τῷ ἱερῷ Θεῷ προσεδρεύουσα, ἡ τότε προφητικῷ παραστᾶσα τῷ Πνεύματι, καὶ παρόντα Χριστὸν τοῖς τότε παροῦσιν ὑπέφαινε. Τοιοῦτος ἦν ὁ Ἀριμαθαῖος Ἰωσὴφ ὁ τῆς ἁπάντων ζωῆς τολμηρὸς ἐνταφιαστὴς, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο μᾶλλον εὐσχήμων βουλευτὴς κηρυττόμενος, ὅτι τὴν μὲν τῶν Ἰουδαίων κατὰ τοῦ Σωτῆρος βουλὴν ὡς αἰσχρὰν παρητήσατο καὶ ἀσχήμονα. «Οὗτος γὰρ, φησὶν, οὐκ ἦν συγκατατιθέμενος τῇ βουλῇ καὶ τῇ γνώμῃ αὐτῶν.» Ταύτην δὲ μόνος αὐτὸς ὡς λαμπρὰν καὶ εὐσχήμονα, εὐσχημόνως καὶ λαμπρῶς ἐβουλεύσατο. Τοιοῦτος καὶ Ζαχαρίας ὁ πρεσβύτης τε καὶ ἱερεὺς ἐγνωρίζετο, ὃς καὶ διὰ τὸ τῆς ἡλικίας ὑπέρακμον καὶ τοῦτο γήρᾳ παρεπομένου θανάτου τὸν ἄδετον δεήσεις ἐκτενεῖς καὶ συχνὰς τῷ Θεῷ προσεκόμιζε μὴ στερηθῆναι τοῦ πᾶσι προσδοκωμένου θεάματος, μηδὲ πρὸ τῆς τούτου θέας τὸν ἐλπιζόμενον καὶ ἐπὶ θύραις ὄντα θεάσασθαι θάνατον. Ταύτην ὁ πρεσβύτης ἀεὶ προσῆγεν τὴν δέησιν· τοῦτο μὲν ἅπασι ταχέως ἐκλάμψαι· τῆς σωτηρίας τὸ φῶς ἐπευχόμενος· τοῦτο δὲ καὶ ἑαυιῷ τὴν τούτου τοῦ φωτὸς ἀνατολὴν ἰδεῖν ἐξαιτούμενος. Καὶ ἵνα μὴ κατὰ τὸν ταύτης αὐτὸν τὸν χρόνον ἀκμάζοντα ταύτης ἁρπαγῆναι τῆς θεωρίας ἀμέτοχον, περὶ τούτου διηνεκῆ τὴν πρεσβείαν ποιούμενος, καὶ μάλιστα ὅτε Θεῷ τὰς κατὰ νόμον λατρείας προσέφερεν, ἵνα τούτων ταχέως ἡ ἄμειψις ἐπὶ τὸ πνευματικώτερον καὶ τελειότερον γένηται, καὶ λυτρωθῶσι τοῦ βάρους οἱ τὸν αὐτὸν ζυγὸν ἐπικείμενον ἔχοντες κλῆροι, κατὰ τὸν τῆς ἐφημερίας καιρὸν τὴν εἰς τὰ Ἅγια τῶν ἁγίων μετὰ θυμιάματος εἴσοδον, ἔνθα τοῦ ἔτους ἅπαξ ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς καὶ μόνον εἰσέτρεχεν, αἷμα ταῖς χερσὶν ἐπαγόμενος καὶ διαῤῥήδην δεικνὺς τῷ αἰνίγματι τὴν ἐσομένην μίαν καὶ μόνην Χριστοῦ τοῦ Σωτῆρος προσένεξιν, ἣν ἑαυτῷ τῷ Θεῷ καὶ Πατρὶ ἀρχιερεὺς καὶ ἀμνὸς ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν εἰς σφαγὴν τῶν ἀνθρώπων προσήγαγεν.

ϛʹ. Ἐνταῦθα γοῦν καὶ Ζαχαρίας γενόμενος (ἦν γὰρ τῆς τοιαύτης λειτουργίας ἐπάξιος), καὶ τῷ θυσιαστηρίῳ παρεστὼς, καὶ περὶ τῆς ἐπιδημίας τοῦ Λόγου τὸν φύσαντα προσδεχθεὶς τῇ ἐπιμονῇ τῆς δεήσεως, δεξιὸν τοῦ θυσιαστηρίου καθ᾿ οὗ τὴν τοῦ θυμιάματος εὐωδίαν προσέφερεν, οὐράνιον ἑστῶτα κατενόησεν ἄγγελον, ἀγγελίαν αὐτῷ δεξιὰν καὶ οὐράνιον φέροντα. Γαβριὴλ οὗτος ἦν ὁ τουτωνὶ τῶν μηνυμάτων διάκονος, ὁ καὶ τῇ προσηγορίᾳ πρὸ τῶν λόγων δηλῶν τὸ ἐξάγγελμα· τὰ γὰρ τῆς τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγου θείας ἐνανθρωπήσεως μηνύσων ἥκει προοίμια, περὶ ἧς καὶ ὁ πρεσβύτης ἐκτενῶς ἐλιτάνευεν· ὃν ἰδὼν ὁ θεσπέσιος ἄγγελος ἐπὶ τῇ ὀπτασίᾳ κλονούμενον, καὶ τῷ φόβῳ τὸν κλόνον συναύξοντα, «Ἐταράχθη γὰρ, φησὶ, Ζαχαρίας ἰδὼν, καὶ φόβος ἐπέπεσεν·» τοῦ νόμου γὰρ ἐν ἑαυτῷ τὸν σάλον εἰκόνιζε, καὶ τὴν πρὸς τὴν εὐαγγελικὴν ὑπετύπου πολιτείαν μετάθεσιν· ἀφαιρεῖται πρῶτον τοῦ φόβου τὸν τάραχον, καὶ εἶθ᾿ οὕτω τῶν Εὐαγγελίων ἀπάρχεται. Οὐ γὰρ φόβου ἦν τὰ λεγόμενα, ἀλλ᾿ ἀφοβίας καὶ τερπνότητος πρόξενα· τί λέγων; «Μὴ φοβοῦ, Ζαχαρία, διότι εἰσηκούσθη ἡ δέησίς σου.»

Such was Simeon, who as a priest received Christ in his arms, and confessed him Master of all 3329creation. Such was Anna, who decently completed that long widowhood, and night and day attended on God in the temple; who then, standing by in the prophetic Spirit, showed forth Christ present to those then present. Such was Joseph of Arimathaea, the daring burier of the life of all, and for this reason proclaimed the more a seemly counsellor, because he refused the counsel of the Jews against the Saviour as shameful and unseemly. «For this man, it says, had not consented to their counsel and their judgement.» But this counsel he alone, as splendid and seemly, counselled seemly and splendidly. Such too was Zacharias known to be, both elder and priest, who, because of the overripeness of his age and of the death that follows on old age [?], brought to God earnest and frequent petitions, not to be deprived of the sight expected by all, nor, before the sight of it, to behold the death that was looked for and stood at the doors. This petition the elder always brought: on the one hand praying that the light of salvation might quickly shine out for all; on the other asking for himself too to see the dawning of this light. And, lest he be snatched away without a share in this sight at the very time when it was flowering, he made unceasing intercession about this, and especially when he offered to God the worships according to the law, that the change of these to the more spiritual and more perfect might come quickly, and the courses that had the same yoke laid upon them might be redeemed from its burden: in the season of their course, the entry with incense into the Holy of Holies, where once a year the high priest alone used to run in, bringing blood in his hands, and plainly showing in the riddle the one and only offering that was to be of Christ the Saviour, which he, high priest and lamb, offered to himself, to God and the Father, for us men, unto slaughter.

6. Here then Zacharias too, having come (for he was worthy of such a ministry), and standing by the altar, and having besought [?] the Begetter concerning the sojourn of the Word with the persistence of his prayer, perceived, at the right of the altar on which he was offering the fragrance of incense, a heavenly angel standing, bearing him tidings of the right hand and from heaven. Gabriel was the minister of these messages, who even by his name, before his words, made the announcement plain; for he has come to announce the preludes of the divine becoming man of God the Word, for which the elder too was earnestly supplicating. Him the divine angel, seeing shaken at the vision, and increasing the shaking with fear — «For Zacharias was troubled, it says, when he saw, and fear fell upon him»; for he was imaging in himself the tossing of the law, and was prefiguring the transfer to the evangelic polity — first takes away the turmoil of fear, and only then begins the good tidings. For the things spoken were not of fear, but procurers of fearlessness and delight. Saying what? «Fear not, Zacharias, for thy prayer is heard.»

Μονονουχὶ βοῶν, Τί δέδοικας, ὦ πρεσβῦτα; τί δέδοικας 3332περὶ ὧν αἰτεῖς λαμβάνων ἀπόκρισιν; τί φοβῇ νομικῆς ἀχθηδόνος λυτρούμενος; τί ταράττει τῆς σκιᾶς ὁρῶν τὴν ἀπόβασιν; τί καταπλήττει τῶν σαλευομένων ὁρῶν τὴν μετάστασιν; φοβερὰ μὲν γάρ εἰσι τὰ ὑπ᾿ ἐμοῦ μηνυόμενα, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ φόβου ποιητικὰ τοῖς ἀκούουσι· καὶ μεγάλα σοι λέγειν ἔχω μυστήρια, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ πρέπει σε τούτων ἀκριβῶς ἀκροώμενον ταραχῇ καὶ φόβῳ συνέχεσθαι· ἀλλὰ χαίρειν σὺν ἐμοὶ καὶ εὐφραίνεσθαι· εὐφροσύνης γὰρ καὶ χαρᾶς ποιητικὰ ταῦτα καθέστηκε. Πάρεστι τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἡ λύτρωσις· ἐλήλυθε τῶν πεπτωκότων ἡ ἔγερσις· τοῦ νόμου τὸ πέρας ἀφίκετο· τῆς χάριτος ὁ καιρὸς ἀνατέταλκε. Καὶ ὄψει τούτων οὐκ εἰς μακρὰν ἐν ὀφθαλμοῖς τὸ κεφάλαιον, τὸν Θεὸν Λόγον ἐκ Παρθένου σαρκούμενον, καὶ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς τοὺς ἀνθρώπους τικτόμενον, καὶ ὅλον τοῦ ἀνθρωπείου γένους τὸ φῦλον ῥυόμενον. Καὶ σὺ τούτων οὐ μόνον ἔσῃ θεατὴς ἅπερ ὁρᾷν ἐπεθύμησας, ἀλλὰ καὶ ὑπηρέτης γενήσῃ μακάριος. Ἵνα δὲ τοῖς ὑπ᾿ ἐμοῦ λεγομένοις πιστεύσειας, καὶ ξένῳ σε θαύματι πρὸς πίστιν ἐπάγομαι, ἐκεῖνά σοι διηγούμενος ἅπερ ὁρᾷν τὸ λοιπὸν οὐκ ἐπίστευες· τίνα ταῦτα; «Ἡ γυνή σου Ἐλισάβετ γεννήσει υἱόν σοι, καὶ καλέσεις τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ Ἰωάννην· καὶ ἔσται χαρά σοι καὶ ἀγαλλίασις, καὶ πολλοὶ ἐπὶ τῇ γεννήσει αὐτοῦ χαρήσονται ἔσται γὰρ μέγας ἐνώπιον Κυρίου, καὶ οἶνον καὶ σίκερα οὐ μὴ πίῃ, καὶ Πνεύματος ἁγίου πλησθήσεται ἐκ κοιλίας μητρὸς αὐτοῦ, καὶ πολλοὺς τῶν υἱῶν Ἰσραὴλ ἐπιστρέψει ἐπὶ Κύριον τὸν Θεὸν αὐτῶν· καὶ αὐτὸς προελεύσεται ἐν Πνεύματι καὶ δυνάμει Ἠλιοῦ, καὶ ἐπιστρέψῃ καρδίας πατέρων ἐπὶ τέκνα, καὶ ἀπειθεῖς ἐν φρονήσει δικαίων, ἑτοιμάσαι Κυρίῳ λαὸν κατεσκευασμένον.»

ζʹ. Ὁρᾷς οἷα τῆς Ἰωάννου καὶ πρὸ αὐτῆς τῆς ἐν κοιλίᾳ συλλήψεως εἶναι μαρτυρεῖται φωναῖς ἀγγελικαῖς τὰ αὐχήματα; Ἐτέχθη μὲν γὰρ ἐκ στείρας καὶ Σαμουὴλ, ἀλλ᾿ οὐκ ἐκ γηραιᾶς οὐδὲ πατρὸς προβεβηκότος καὶ γέροντος. Ἐτέχθη καὶ Ἰσαὰκ ἐκ γηραιῶν μὲν καὶ τοῦ τίκτειν ἐνηλλαχότων τὸν καιρὸν καὶ τὴν δύναμιν· ἀλλ᾿ οὐ Πνεύματος ἁγίου ἐκ νηδύος αὐτῆς ἐνεπίμπλατο· ἀλλ᾿ οὔτε τοῖς ἄλλοις ἅπασιν τούτων οὐδεὶς ὡραΐζετο, οἷς Ἰωάννης καὶ πρὸ τοκετῶν ὡραΐζεται. Καὶ προφήτης μὲν ἦν ὁ Σαμουὴλ, καὶ ὡς βλέπων ἀληθῶς ἐχρημάτιζε· ἔβλεπεν γὰρ ὡς παρόντα τὰ μέλλοντα καὶ οἶνον οὐ πέπωκεν οὐδὲ σίκερα, ἀλλ᾿ ἔρημον οὐ κατῴκησεν οὐδὲ ξέναις ἀνθρώποις τροφαῖς διετρέφετο· ἀλλ᾿ οὔτε γεννώμενος χαρὰν πολλοῖς ἐνεποίησεν, ὥσπερ Ἰωάννης ὁ μέγιστος ἔδραμε τῆς παγκοσμίου χαρᾶς προγεννώμενος ἡγούμενος.

All but crying: Why art thou afraid, O elder? 3332Why art thou afraid, receiving an answer concerning what thou askest? Why dost thou fear, being redeemed from the burden of the law? Why does it trouble thee, seeing the passing of the shadow? Why does it dismay thee, seeing the removal of the things that are shaken? For fearful indeed are the things announced by me, but not productive of fear in those who hear; and I have great mysteries to tell thee, but it is not fitting that thou, listening closely to them, be held by trouble and fear, but rejoice with me and be glad; for these are productive of gladness and joy. The redemption of men is at hand; the raising of the fallen has come; the end of the law has arrived; the season of grace has dawned. And before long thou shalt see in thine eyes the sum of these: God the Word made flesh of a Virgin, and born like us men, and rescuing the whole race of mankind. And of these things which thou didst desire to see thou shalt not only be a beholder, but thou shalt also become a blessed servant. And that thou mayest believe what is said by me, I lead thee by a strange wonder also to faith, telling thee those things which thou didst no longer believe thou wouldst see. What are these? «Thy wife Elisabeth shall bear thee a son, and thou shalt call his name John; and thou shalt have joy and gladness, and many shall rejoice at his birth: for he shall be great before the Lord, and shall drink neither wine nor strong drink; and he shall be filled with the Holy Spirit from his mother's womb, and many of the sons of Israel shall he turn to the Lord their God; and he shall go before in the Spirit and power of Elias, to turn the hearts of fathers to children, and the disobedient to the prudence of the just, to make ready for the Lord a people prepared.»

7. Seest thou what are the glories of John, attested by angelic voices even before his very conception in the womb? For Samuel too was born of a barren woman, but not of an aged one, nor of a father advanced and old. Isaac too was born of aged parents, who had passed the season and the power of bearing; but he was not filled with the Holy Spirit from her womb. But none of these either was adorned with all the other things with which John is adorned even before his birth. And Samuel was a prophet, and was truly styled a seer, for he saw the things to come as present; and he drank no wine nor strong drink; but he did not dwell in the desert, nor was he nourished on foods strange to men; nor at his birth did he cause joy to many, as John the greatest ran, born beforehand as leader of the joy of the whole world.

Ἐγεγόνει στείρας καρπὸς καὶ Ἰωσὴφ ὁ σοφώτατος· καὶ πρὸ τοῦ γε ὁ ἐκ στείρας αὐτὸν Ῥαχὴλ γεννησάμενος Ἰακὼβ ὁ μακάριος· καὶ σώφρων μὲν ἦν Ἰωσὴφ (ἔστησε γὰρ κατὰ τῆς Αἰγυπτίας τὸ τῆς σωφροσύνης ἀξιάγαστον τρόπαιον), ἀλλ᾿ οὐ παρθενίας στρατηγὸς ὡς Ἰωάννης ἐγένετο· ὅσον δὲ παρθενίας καὶ σωφροσύνης τὸ μεταξὺ, αὐτὰ βοᾷ τὰ τῶν ἀρετῶν ἀξιώματα· καὶ ὁ κρείττων υἱῶν καὶ θυγατέρων τόπος, ὁ μόνοις ἐκεῖσε διδόμενος τοῖς ψυχῆς ὁμοῦ 3333καὶ σαρκὸς τὴν ἐν Χριστῷ παρθενίαν τιμήσασι, καὶ μιμηταῖς τῆς τοῦτον τεκούσης ἀχράντου Παρθένου γενομένοις τε καὶ ὑπάρξασιν. Τοῦτο δὲ καὶ αὐτὸς ὁ Σωτὴρ πᾶσιν ἐπιφανὲς ἐφανέρωσε, μὴ πάντας εἰπὼν τούτου τυγχάνειν χωρητικοὺς, τοῦ τῆς παρθενίας, λέγω, χαρίσματος, ἀλλὰ μόνους ἐκείνους ὁ Ἰησοῦς ὡς ἀξίους κατὰ θείαν ηὐτρέπισται πρόγνωσιν. Ἠγαπήθη Θεῷ καὶ Ἰακὼβ ἔτι κατὰ τὴν μητρῴαν νηδὺν διαιτώμενος· ἐῤῥέθη γὰρ θείᾳ φωνῇ πρὸς τὴν τοῦτον ἐγκόλπιον φέρουσαν, «Τὸν Ἰακὼβ ἠγάπησα, καὶ τὸν Ἡσαῦ ἐμίσησα·» ἀλλ᾿ οὐ Πνεύματος ἁγίου κυοφορούμενος γέγονεν ἔμπλεως· καὶ Θεῷ παλαίειν παννύχιος ἴσχυσε· νὺξ γὰρ ἀγνοίας τότε Θεοῦ τοῖς πᾶσιν ἐκέχυτο· ἀλλ᾿ οὐ Θεοῦ προτρέχειν καὶ Θεὸν βαπτίζειν δεδύνητο, ὃ μόνος ἐν ἀνθρώποις Ἰωάννης ποιεῖν ἀπετόλμησεν. Ὄρθρος γὰρ ὁ τῆς θεογνωσίας ἀναβεβήκει, λοιπὸν καὶ τῆς ἀληθοῦς ἡμέρας ἡ φαιδρότης ἐπέλαμπε· κἀκ τῆς πάλης ὁ ἀπάλαιστος τῷ μηρῷ διὰ τοὺς ἐξ αὐτοῦ ἐκ μηροῦ γεννησομένους, ἐπέσκαζε, καὶ ὅλως ὀξυδρομεῖν οὐκ ἠδύνατο, προμηνύων αὐτῶν ἐμφανῶς τῷ αἰνίγματι, τὸ πρὸς τὴν θείαν χάριν ἐπιδημοῦσαν τῷ βίῳ βραδὺ καὶ δυσκίνητον καὶ τὴν ἐκ τούτου φανησομένην αὐτῶν πανολέθριον χώλασιν, ἣν Ἰωάννης παντελῶς οὐχ ὑπέμεινε, καίτοι κατ᾿ ἐκείνους ἐξ Ἰακὼβ καταγόμενος· ἀλλ᾿ εὔδρομός τις πρὸς ταύτην καὶ Πρόδρομος γίγνεται τῆς Ἰουδαϊκῆς ἀπιστίας, καὶ νυκτὸς πόῤῥω καταλείπων τὰ μυστήρια.

ηʹ. Ἠβουλόμην δὲ καὶ τὸν Σαμψὼν παραγαγεῖν εἰς παράθεσιν. Ἐκ στείρας γὰρ καὶ αὐτὸς ἐγεγέννητο, καὶ δεήσεως τῆς πρὸς Θεὸν βλαστὸς ἀναπέφηνε, καὶ τὴν κεφαλὴν ἑνὼν οὐκ ἐκείρατο· «Ξυρὸν γὰρ, φησὶν, οὐκ ἀνέβη ἐπὶ τὴν κεφαλὴν αὐτοῦ·» καὶ ἄλλοις δὲ θείοις τισὶν ἐκόμα δωρήμασιν· ἀλλ᾿ ἡ Δαλιδὰ καὶ νῦν τὸ πορνίδιον πάλιν αὐτὸν γενναίως κρατήσασα ἀκολουθεῖν ἡμῖν πρὸς Ἰωάννην οὐκ εἴασεν. Καὶ μεγάλοι μὲν ἐνώπιον Κυρίου πάντες ἐτύγχανον, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ πολλοὺς τῶν υἱῶν Ἰσραὴλ ἐπὶ Κύριον τὸν Θεὸν αὐτῶν ἐπιστρέψαντες φαίνονται· οὔτε Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ προωδεύκασιν ἐν Ἠλιοῦ δυνάμει καὶ πνεύματι, οὔτε καρδίας πατέρων ἐπὶ τέκνα τῶν ἐν νόμῳ τραφέντων ἐπὶ τοὺς γεγονότας ἐν χάριτι μεθαρμόσαντες δείκνυνται, οὐδ᾿ ἀπειθείας Ἰουδαίους ἐν φρονήσει δικαίων τῶν ἐν Χριστῷ δεδικαιωμένων ὡδήγησαν, οὔτε Κυρίῳ ἐπὶ γῆς ἀναλάμψαντι λαὸν κατεσκευασμένον ἡτοίμασαν, οὔτε τὰ τούτων ἐσχήκασιν μείζονα. Τί γὰρ μεῖζον τοῦ ἰδεῖν ἐν σαρκὶ καὶ βαπτίσαι τὸν Κύριον ὕδασιν, καὶ ἐκ στείρων ἔσχον μητέρων τὴν γέννησιν; καὶ ἄλλοις τισὶ πλεονεκτήμασιν ἔθαλλον, μεγάλοις μὲν εἰ πρὸς ἑτέρων ἀρετὰς παραβάλλοιντο, μικροῖς δὲ λίαν εἰ τοῖς Ἰωάννου χαρίσμασι τολμοῖεν συγκρίνεσθαι.

Joseph the most wise too was the fruit of a barren woman; and before him, at least, blessed Jacob, who begot him of the barren Rachel; and Joseph was chaste (for he set up against the Egyptian woman the admirable trophy of chastity), but he did not become a general of virginity, as John did; and how great is the distance between virginity and chastity the very dignities of the virtues cry out, and the place better than sons and daughters, which is given there only to those who have honoured 3333virginity in Christ in soul and flesh together, and have become and been imitators of the undefiled Virgin who bore him. And this the Saviour himself made manifest, plain to all, saying that not all are capable of this — of the gift of virginity, I mean — but only those whom Jesus has prepared as worthy according to divine foreknowledge. Jacob too was loved by God while still living in his mother's womb; for it was said by a divine voice to her who carried him in her bosom, «Jacob have I loved, and Esau have I hated»; but he did not become full of the Holy Spirit while he was carried in the womb. And he had the strength to wrestle with God all night long — for a night of ignorance of God was then poured over all — but he had not been able to run before God and to baptize God, which John alone among men dared to do. For the dawn of the knowledge of God had risen, and thereafter the brightness of the true day was shining; and from the wrestling the unthrown one limped on his thigh, because of those who would be born from him out of his thigh, and could not run swiftly at all, foretelling plainly in the riddle their slowness and sluggishness towards divine grace when it sojourned in life, and the all-destroying lameness that would appear in them from this; which John did not undergo at all, although descended from Jacob as they were; but he becomes a swift runner towards it, and Forerunner, leaving far behind the mysteries of the Jewish unbelief and of night.

8. I wished also to bring Samson forward for comparison. For he too had been born of a barren woman, and appeared as the shoot of prayer to God, and of his own will did not shear his head — «For a razor, it says, did not come up upon his head» — and he wore as long locks certain other divine gifts too; but Delilah, the little harlot, overpowering him nobly even now once more, did not allow him to follow us to John. And all were great before the Lord, but they do not appear to have turned many of the sons of Israel to the Lord their God; nor did they go before Christ God in the power and spirit of Elias, nor are they shown to have transposed the hearts of fathers to children — of those reared under the law to those born in grace — nor did they guide the Jews of disobedience to the prudence of the just, those justified in Christ, nor did they prepare a people made ready for the Lord when he shone forth on earth, nor did they have things greater than these. For what is greater than to see the Lord in the flesh and to baptize him with waters — and they had their birth from barren mothers? And they flourished with certain other advantages, great if they should be compared with the virtues of others, but very small if they should dare to be measured against the gifts of John.

Πρὸς ἃ δι᾿ ὑπερβολὴν ἀνυπέρβλητον καὶ Ζαχαρίας τότε ὁ μέλλων Ἰωάννου πατὴρ ἀναδείκνυσθαι τὸν νοῦν ὡς ἐπ᾿ ἀμηχάνοις τισὶν ἐκπληττόμενος, τῆς ἀπιστίας τὸ κέντρον ἐδέχετο· καὶ δὴ τούτῳ πληγεὶς πρὸς τὸν μακάριον ἔφασκεν ἄγγελον ἐκεῖνα τῆς δεινης 3336ἀπιστίας τὰ ῥήματα· «Κατὰ τί γνώσομαι τοῦτο; ἐγὼ γάρ εἰμι πρεσβύτης, καὶ ἡ γυνή μου προβεβηκυῖα ἐν ταῖς ἡμέραις αὐτῆς·» ἅπερ εἰπεῖν Ἰωάννου τὸν πατέρα, παντελῶς οὐκ ἔπρεπεν· εἴρηκεν δὲ οὐχ ὁ Ἰωάννου πατὴρ τῆς μεγάλα τε καὶ ἐξαίσια βοώσης φωνῆς, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς τοῦ στενοφώνου καὶ βραδυγλώσσου νόμου φέρων τὸ πρόσωπον· στενόφωνος γὰρ ὁ Μωϋσῆς καὶ βραδύγλωσσος ὁ τοῦτον γεγραφὼς ἀναγέγραπται· ὅθεν εὐθὺς καὶ ᾧ πᾶν ἐπετάττετο, καὶ εἰκονίζειν ἐν ἑαυτῷ νόμου τοῦ Μωσαϊκοῦ τὴν σιώπησιν, ἣν ἐπιφανέντος ἡμῖν ἐν σαρκὶ τοῦ μεγάλου νομοθέτου Χριστοῦ σεσιώπηκεν, ὡς νόμου βουληθεὶς γενέσθαι προτύπωμα· ἐπείθετο γὰρ τοῦ ἀγγέλου τὴν σιωπὴν αὐτοῦ καθαρίζοντος· «Ἐγώ εἰμι Γαβριὴλ ὁ παρεστηκὼς ἐνώπιον τοῦ Θεοῦ, καὶ ἀπεστάλην λαλῆσαιπρὸς σὲ καὶ εὐαγγελίσασθαί σοι ταῦτα· καὶ ἰδοὺ ἔσῃ σιωπῶν, καὶ μὴ δυνάμενος λαλῆσαι ἄχρι ἧς ἡμέρας γένηται ταῦτα, ἀνθ᾿ ὧν οὐκ ἐπίστευσας τοῖς λόγοις μου, οἵτινες πληρωθήσονται εἰς τὸν καιρὸν αὐτῶν·» καὶ μάλα εἰκότως ἐπιτιμᾷ τὸ ἄφωνον, εἰ καὶ τῆς φωνῆς γεννήτωρ ἤμελλεν γενέσθαι· οὐ μόνον ὅτι τῶν ἐν νόμῳ διαπιστούντων γενέσθαι τύπος ἠνέσχετο, ἀλλ᾿ ὅτι μηδὲ τὴν ταῦτα κηρύττειν στελλομένην φωνὴν ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ προϊέναι πεπίστευκεν, καὶ τῇ τῆς φωνῆς ἀπιστήσας προόδῳ, δικαίως τῆς φωνῆς ἐστερίσκετο. Ἐπειδὴ δὲ, «Δι᾿ ὧν τις ἁμαρτάνει, διὰ τούτων, ὡς ὁ σοφός φησιν Σολομὼν, κολάζεται,» ἵνα μάθοι τῇ ἐξ ἐρήμου προϊούσῃ φωνῇ μὴ ἀπιστεῖν· ἐξ ἀγόνου γὰρ γῆς ἤτοι μήτρας ὁ Ἰωάννης ἐξέθορε, καὶ ἐν ἐρήμῳ βοώσῃ ταῖς Ἰουδαίων πίστεως καρπὸν οὐ τικτούσαις ψυχαῖς, ἢ καὶ τῇ ἐξ ἐθνῶν Ἐκκλησίᾳ ὡς καρπὸν οὐκ ἐχούσῃ τὸ πρότερον ἥμερόν τε καὶ τρόφιμον καὶ τρέφειν Χριστὸν κατὰ τὴν ἐν Σαμαρείᾳ βρῶσιν δυνάμενον· ἑκατέρῳ γὰρ τούτων Ἰωάννης ἐν ἐρήμῳ ἡ μεγάλη καὶ μεγάλα βοῶσα φωνὴ τὸ σωτήριον ὄντως βεβόηκεν κήρυγμα, τὸ καὶ καρποφόρον εἰς γῆν μεταβαλεῖν ἑκατέραν ἱκανὸν ἄκαρπον ἔρημον.

θʹ. Τί γὰρ ἄρα καὶ ὁ πρεσβύτης ἐκεῖνος καὶ ἱερεὺς καὶ τοῦ νόμου παιδευτὴς καὶ διδάσκαλος, καὶ τὴν παράκλησιν τοῦ Ἰσραὴλ προσδεχόμενος, καὶ ὑπὲρ ταύτης ἀεὶ προσάγων σύντονον δέησιν, μὴ τοῖς ταύτης εὐαγγελίοις ἐπείθετο, εἰ μὴ τὸν ὑπὸ νόμον ἐν ἑαυτῷ προεζωγράφει ἄπιστον; Εἰ μὲν γὰρ ἄγγελον ἅγιον ᾤετο τὸν τῷ θυσιαστηρίῳ παρεστῶτα, καὶ ταῦτα πρὸς αὐτὸν ἐκεῖθεν φθεγγόμενον, διὰ τί μετὰ πίστεως τῶν λεγομένων οὐκ ἤκουε, τῷ κατὰ τὸν Ἀβραὰμ καὶ τὴν Σάῤῥαν πρὸς τὸ πιστεῦσαιῥυθμίζων ἑαυτὸν ὑποδείγματι; καίτοι εἰ καὶ τοῦτο μὴ ἦν, ἔδει πάντως Θεῷ πιστεῦσαι θεσπίσαντι ξένον τι καὶ ὑπὲρ φύσιν ἐργάσασθαι· τί γὰρ ἐν τοῖς οὖσίν ἐστιν ὃ μὴ θάτερον πρὸς ὕπαρξιν τῷ τοῦ Θεοῦ πανσθενεῖ συντρέχει προστάγματι;

At which, through a surpassing beyond all surpassing, Zacharias too, who was then about to be shown the father of John, struck in mind as at things inconceivable, received the sting of unbelief; and indeed, smitten with it, he said to the blessed angel those words of dread 3336unbelief: «Whereby shall I know this? for I am an old man, and my wife well stricken in her days»; which it was altogether unfitting for John's father to say. But he spoke them not as the father of the voice crying great and extraordinary things, but as bearing the person of the narrow-voiced and slow-tongued law; for Moses, who wrote it, is recorded as narrow-voiced and slow-tongued: whence straightway it was his, to whom everything was enjoined, also to image in himself the silencing of the Mosaic law, which fell silent when Christ, the great Lawgiver, appeared to us in the flesh, as having willed to become a prefiguring of the law. For he obeyed the angel purifying his silence: «I am Gabriel, that stand in the presence of God; and I was sent to speak unto thee, and to bring thee these good tidings; and behold, thou shalt be silent, and not able to speak, until the day that these things come to pass, because thou believedst not my words, which shall be fulfilled in their season.» And very fittingly he lays voicelessness on him as a penalty, even though he was to become the begetter of the Voice: not only because he consented to become a type of those under the law who disbelieve, but because he did not believe that the voice sent to proclaim these things would come forth from him; and, having disbelieved the coming forth of the voice, he was justly deprived of voice. And since «By what things a man sins, by these, as the wise Solomon says, is he punished,» that he might learn not to disbelieve the voice coming forth from the desert — for John leapt forth from barren ground, that is, a womb — and crying in the desert to the souls of the Jews that bore no fruit of faith, or also to the Church from the Gentiles, as having formerly no fruit both cultivated and nourishing, and able to nourish Christ, after the food at Samaria; for to each of these John in the desert, the great voice and crying great things, has truly cried the saving proclamation, which is able to change each barren desert into fruitful land.

9. For why then did that elder — both priest, and trainer and teacher of the law, and awaiting the consolation of Israel, and ever offering earnest prayer for it — not believe its good tidings, unless he was portraying beforehand in himself the unbeliever under the law? For if he thought him a holy angel who stood by the altar and uttered these things to him from there, why did he not hear what was said with faith, ordering himself for belief by the example of Abraham and Sarah? And yet, even if this were not so, he ought by all means to have believed God when he decreed that he would work something strange and above nature: for what is there among the things that are which does not run together into existence more quickly at the all-powerful command of God? This Job too, the wonderful, being taught by God himself through the voice that persuaded [?] him in the cloud, wondrously cried out: «I know that thou canst do all things, and nothing is impossible to thee.»

ὃ καὶ Ἰὼβ ὁ θαυμάσιος ὑπ᾿ αὐτοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ διὰ τῆς πρὸς αὐτὸν ἐν τῷ νέφει πειθομένης διδασκόμενος, θαυμασίως πως ἀνεβόησε· «Οἶδα ὅτι πάντα δύνασαι, ἀδυνατεῖ δέ σοι οὐδέν·» εἰ δὲ δύναμιν ἐχθράν τινα καὶ ἀντίθεον τὴν πρὸς αὐτὸν φθεγγομένην ἐτόπαζε, τί τῶν λαληθέντων 3337ἐπεζήτει τὴν πίστωσιν, λέγων, Κατὰ τί γνώσομαι τοῦτο; εἰδὼς ὡς ψεύστης ἐστὶν ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς, καὶ ἀλήθειαν λαλεῖν οὐκ ἐπίσταται· καὶ ὡς τὸ πρῶτον ψευσάμενον, καὶ ὅλως δεχθεὶς ἐν τῷ ψεύσματι, οὐκ ἀπορήσει καὶ τὸ δεύτερον ψεύσασθαι· ὅπως μείζοσιν ἀπάταις αἰχμάλωτον τὸν ἁλόντα ταῖς πρώταις αὐτοῦ προσβολαῖς ἀπενέγκοιτο· ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τοιοῦτός τις εὐήθης ὁ Ζαχαρίας ὁ Ἰωάννου τοῦ μεγάλου πατὴρ ἐτύγχανεν, ἄπαγε· οὐδ᾿ ἐκ ῥίζης τοιαύτης οὕτω μωρᾶς ὁ γλυκύτατος οὗτος καρπὸς Ἰωάννης ἐφύετο, ὁ καὶ διὰ τοῦτο τάχα τρεφόμενος μέλιτι· ὅτι πᾶσι τοῖς τὴν ἐξαίρετον ἀγαπῶσι ζωὴν ἡδύς ἐστιν καὶ γλυκύτατος, πρὸς τὴν οἰκείαν αὐτοὺς δι᾿ ἔνθεον ἡδονὴν προσκαλεῖταί τε καὶ προτρέπεται μίμησιν· ἀλλ᾿ ἔκστασιν ὁ πρεσβύτης, ὡς ἔφημεν, οἶμαι, κατὰ τὸν Ἰσαὰκ ἐκεῖνον ὑπέμεινεν, ἵνα τοῦ μὲν νόμου τὴν σιγὴν, καὶ τὸν ὑπὸ νόμον προδηλώσοι τὸ ἄπιστον· τοῦ δὲ Εὐαγγελίου τὴν βροντὴν τὴν πάντα τῆς οἰκουμένης κατακτυποῦσαν τὰ πέρατα, καὶ τῶν ὑπὸ χάριν ἐθνῶν τὸ πιστὸν προμηνύσειεν.

ιʹ. Οὐκοῦν μέγας ὁ ἱερεὺς καὶ μυστικῶς ἀπιστεῖ, καὶ μυστικώτερον ἀφαιρεῖται τοῦ λόγου τὴν πρόοδον διὰ τὸ τῆς Ἰουδαϊκῆς ἀπιστίας ἀλόγιστον· κἀκ τούτου Ἰωάννης ὁ τοῦ Λόγου πρόεισι Πρόδρομος φέρων ἐν ἑαυτῷ τὴν ἀρετὴν τοῦ γεννήτορος· εἴπερ ἐκ τοῦ καρποῦ τὸ δένδρον γιγνώσκεται, καὶ οὐ δύναται δένδρον σαπρὸν καρπὸν καλὸν ποιεῖν, κατὰ τὴν θείαν ἐκείνην φωνὴν καὶ θεότευκτον· εἰ γὰρ μὴ οὕτως τις τοῦ πρεσβύτου τὴν ἀπιστίαν νοεῖν ἐθελήσειεν, ἔσται πολὺ τῆς Ἐλισάβετ ἐν ἀρεταῖς ἐλαττούμενος· ἐκείνη γὰρ καίτοι τούτων μηδαμῶς ἐπακούσασα (ἄναυδος γὰρ ὡς αὐτὴν ὁ πρεσβύτης), ἀνέλυσε τὴν μακαρίαν Παρθένον ἐλθοῦσαν πρὸς αὐτὴν θεωρήσασα, καὶ τῆς παρθενικῆς πυθομένη προσρήσεως, Θεοτόκον αὐτὴν εὐθὺς ἀνηγόρευσε· «Εὐλογημένη σὺ ἐν γυναιξὶν,» μεγάλῃ βοῶσα τῇ φωνῇ,«καὶ εὐλογημένος ὁ καρπὸς τῆς κοιλίας σου. Καὶ πόθεν μοι τοῦτο ἵνα ἔλθῃ ἡ μήτηρ τοῦ Κυρίου μου πρός με; ἰδοὺ γὰρ ὡς ἐγένετο ἡ φωνὴ τοῦ ἀσπασμοῦ σου εἰς τὰ ὦτά μου, ἐσκίρτησεν τὸ βρέφος ἐν ἀγαλλιάσει ἐν τῇ κοιλίᾳ μου,» ἣν καὶ ἀξίως ἐμακάριζεν λέγουσα, καὶ «Μακαρία ἡ πιστεύσασα, ὅτι ἔστι τελείωσις τοῖς λελαλημένοις αὐτῇ παρὰ Κυρίου.»

But if he supposed it some hostile and God-opposed power that spoke to him, why did he seek 3337the assurance of what was spoken, saying, Whereby shall I know this? — knowing that he is a liar from the beginning, and does not know how to speak truth; and that, having lied the first time and been shown [?] wholly in the lie, he will not be at a loss to lie the second time also, so that he may carry off with greater deceits as a captive the one taken by his first assaults. But Zacharias, the father of the great John, was not such a simpleton — away with it! Nor from a root so foolish did this sweetest fruit John grow, who perhaps for this very reason was also fed on honey: because to all who love the choice life he is pleasant and most sweet, and calls and urges them, through a godly pleasure, to the imitation of himself. But the elder, as we said, I think, underwent an ecstasy like that Isaac, that he might show beforehand the silence of the law and the unbelief of those under the law, and foretell the thunder of the Gospel that resounds through all the ends of the world, and the faith of the Gentiles under grace.

10. So then the great priest both disbelieves mystically, and more mystically still is deprived of the going-forth of speech, on account of the irrationality of Jewish unbelief; and from this John, the Forerunner of the Word, comes forth, bearing in himself the virtue of his begetter; if indeed the tree is known from its fruit, and a rotten tree cannot make good fruit, according to that divine and God-wrought voice. For if anyone should not be willing to understand the old man's unbelief in this way, he will be much inferior in virtues to Elisabeth. For she, although she had in no way heard these things (for the old man was speechless towards her), [?] on seeing the blessed Virgin come to her, and on learning of the virginal greeting, at once proclaimed her Theotokos: «Blessed art thou among women,» crying out with a loud voice, «and blessed is the fruit of thy womb. And whence is this to me, that the mother of my Lord should come to me? For behold, as the voice of thy greeting came into my ears, the babe leapt in exultation in my womb»; and she worthily called her blessed, saying also: «Blessed is she that believed, for there is a fulfilment to the things spoken to her from the Lord.» For not only did she recognise by the Spirit that she was with child, but she was also rich in the knowledge of the things spoken to her from God by an angel's voice; which would be altogether incredible and implausible.

Οὐ μόνον γὰρ ἔγκυον αὐτὴν οὖσαν ἐπέγνω τῷ Πνεύματι, ἀλλὰ καὶ τῶν παρὰ Θεοῦ λελαλημένων αὐτῇ δι᾿ ἀγγέλου φωνῆς ἐπλούτει τὴν εἴδησιν· ὅπερ ἄπιστον ἂν εἴη παντελῶς καὶ ἀπίθανον· δείκνυσι γὰρ Ζαχαρίου τὴν πρὸς Θεὸν τελειότητα, οὐ μόνον τὸ πρὸς αὐτὸν ἀπεστάλθαι τὸν ἄγγελον τὸν τὰ τοιαῦτα κατακοσμήσαντα ἐπὶ γῆς ἐπαγγελίᾳ, καὶ τὸ Ἰωάννου τοῦ Βαπτιστοῦ γενέσθαι, τὸ τῇ Χριστοῦ συγγενείᾳ σεμνύνεσθαι· «Ἰδοὺ γὰρ, φησὶν, Ἐλισάβετ ἡ συγγενής σου,» αὐτὸς ὁ τὴν ἁγίαν Παρθένον εὐαγγελισάμενος ἄγγελος· ἀλλὰ καὶ ἐξ ὧν μετὰ τὸν Ἰωάννου τόκον προφητικῶς μεμελῴδηκεν, καὶ τὰ περὶ Χριστοῦ προδιήγγειλεν, «Εὐλογητὸς, βοῶν, Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς τοῦ Ἰσραὴλ ὅτι ἐπεσκέψατο καὶ ἐποίησεν λύτρωσιν τῷ λαῷ αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἤγειρεν κέρας σωτηρίας ἡμῖν, ἐν οἴκῳ Δαβὶδ τοῦ παιδὸς αὐτοῦ, καθὼς ἐλάλησε διὰ στόματος τῶν ἁγίων τῶν ἀπ᾿ αἰῶνος προφητῶν αὐτοῦ·» καὶ τὰ ἕτερα ὅσα περὶ αὐτοῦ τούτοις ἐπήγαγεν, ἃ καὶ πρὸ θανάτου θεάσασθαι ἐ̔κάστης ὥρας προσηύχετο.

3340ιαʹ. Ὁμοίως δὲ καὶ ἀφ᾿ ὧν πρὸς τῷ τοῦ ᾄσματος πέρατι περὶ τοῦ οἰκείου παιδὸς προεφήτευσεν· «Καὶ σὺ, παιδίον, λέγων, προφήτης Ὑψίστου κληθήσῃ· προπορεύσῃ γὰρ πρὸ προσώπου Κυρίου ἑτοιμάσαι ὁδοὺς αὐτοῦ τοῦ δοῦναι γνῶσιν σωτηρίας τῷ λαῷ αὐτοῦ, ἐν ἀφέσει ἁμαρτιῶν ἡμῶν, διὰ σπλάγχνα ἐλέους Θεοῦ ἡμῶν.» Ἅπερ οὐκ ἀπὸ καρδίας ἀλλὰ Πνεύματος ἁγίου γενόμενος ἔμπλεος ἔλεγε· γέγραπται γὰρ ἐν Εὐαγγελίοις ὧδε περὶ αὐτοῦ· «Καὶ Ζαχαρίας, φησὶν, ὁ πατὴρ αὐτοῦ ἐπλήσθη Πνεύματος ἁγίου, καὶ προεφήτευσεν λέγων·» τί λέγων; ταῦτα δηλαδὴ ἅπερ προλαβόντες εἰρήκαμεν· πρόδηλον δὲ ὡς οὐκ ἂν οὗτος ὁ Ζαχαρίας ἁγίου σαφῶς ἐνεπίμπλατο Πνεύματος, εἰ μὴ πιστός τις ὑπῆρχε τῷ Πνεύματι καὶ τῆς εὐκταίας αὐτοῦ πληρώσεως ἄξιος· καὶ ὡς οὐδὲ Ἰωάννου πατὴρ ἐχρημάτιζε, εἰ μὴ καὶ ταύτης ἦν τῆς ἀξίας ἐφάμιλλος.

For it shows the perfection of Zacharias towards God, not only in the angel's having been sent to him, the angel who set such things in order on earth by a promise, and in his becoming [?] of John the Baptist, and in his being dignified with kinship to Christ — «For behold,» says the very angel who brought the good tidings to the holy Virgin, «Elisabeth thy kinswoman» — but also from the things he sang prophetically after John's birth, when he foretold what concerned Christ, crying: «Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, for he hath visited and wrought redemption for his people, and hath raised up a horn of salvation for us in the house of his servant David, as he spake by the mouth of his holy prophets which have been since the world began»; and the other things that he added to these concerning him, which he also prayed every hour to behold before death.

334011. In like manner too from what he prophesied at the end of the song concerning his own child: «And thou, child,» he says, «shalt be called the prophet of the Most High: for thou shalt go before the face of the Lord to prepare his ways; to give knowledge of salvation to his people in the remission of our sins, through the bowels of mercy of our God.» These things he spoke not from the heart, but being made full of the Holy Spirit: for it is written thus of him in the Gospels: «And Zacharias,» it says, «his father was filled with the Holy Spirit, and prophesied, saying» — saying what? These things, plainly, which we have spoken of before. And it is quite plain that this Zacharias would not clearly have been filled with the Holy Spirit unless he had been someone faithful to the Spirit and worthy of his longed-for filling; and that he would not have been styled the father of John either, unless he had been a match for this dignity too. Let all these things, then, lead the way to John's conception, showing him, before his conception, greater than all among those born of women; even as Christ, the truth, truly bore him this witness too; lest some should suppose him, on this ground — that he was not born of any great persons — less than any one of men. And this the evangelic writing too, taking care for it, as I think, by this Scripture concerning him set forth beforehand, proscribed in advance, the suspicion of those who would suspect this, saying plainly: «And they were [?] both walking before God in all the commandments and ordinances of the Lord blameless.» What then? That Zacharias and Elisabeth, John's begetters, should have won at that time such an evangelic vote, bearing witness to them of the citadel of the virtues — this is the surpassing thing to behold among men. For the unbribed vote not only granted them the just, but added also the «before God»: for they studied to appear just to God alone by evangelic laws, foreseeing these with a prophetic eye as already laid down; and to attempt the way of life of «the left hand hiding the right», anticipating in work and deed the precept of such legislation. And to the vote this too is clearly added: the blameless fulfilment of all the Mosaic commandments and ordinances together, to show those who have this lawful verdict as both of them with both — crowned with the boasts of the old legislation, and made brilliant with the heights of the evangelic way of life.

12. For such it was needful and fitting that John's progenitors too should be seen; since he who was to come from them was to be shown to us as mediator of each Testament, and truly to hold together in himself what is choice in both. For it is confessed that he was marked out as the end of the Elder (for he himself completed the number of the prophetic choir, since all the prophets prophesied until John the Baptist), and as the beginning and prelude of the Newer: for he led the way before all the apostles; since he was sent to go before and to run before Christ, with a holy [?] angelic dignity.

Ταῦτα μὲν οὖν ἅπαντα τῆς Ἰωάννου καθηγείσθω συλλήψεως, δεικνύντα πάντων αὐτὸν τῶν ἐν γεννητοῖς γυναικῶν πρὸ συλλήψεως μείζονα· καθὰ καὶ ταύτην αὐτῷ Χριστὸς ἡ ἀλήθεια τὴν μαρτυρίαν ἀληθῶς μεμαρτύρηκε· ἵνα μὴ κατὰ τοῦτό τινες τὸ μὴ ἐκ μεγάλων τετέχθαι τινῶν ὑπολάβοιεν αὐτόν τινος τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἐλάττονα· ὃ καὶ τὸ γράμμα τὸ εὐαγγελικὸν διὰ φροντίδος, ὡς οἶμαι, τιθέμενον διὰ τῆσδε τῆς περὶ αὐτὸν προεκτεθείσης Γραφῆς τὴν ὑπόνοιαν τῶν τοῦτο μελλόντων ὑπονοεῖν προαπεκήρυξε φῆσαν ἐναργῶς· «Ἦσαν δὲ καὶ οἱ ἀμφότεροι ἐνώπιον τοῦ Θεοῦ πορευόμενοι ἐν πάσαις ταῖς ἐντολαῖς καὶ δικαιώμασι τοῦ Κυρίου ἄμεμπτοι·» τί δέ; τοῦ τοιαύτην ἑλεῖν τότε Ζαχαρίαν καὶ τὴν Ἐλισάβετ τοὺς Ἰωάννου γεννήτορας ψῆφον εὐαγγελικὴν μαρτυροῦσαν αὐτοῖς τῶν ἀρετῶν τὴν ἀκρόπολιν, τὸ ὑπερέχον ἐστὶν ἐν ἀνθρώποις θεάσασθαι· οὐ μόνον γὰρ αὐτοῖς τὸ δίκαιον ἡ ἀδέκαστος ψῆφος παρέσχετο, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὸ ἐνώπιον τοῦ Θεοῦ προσήγαγεν· Θεῷ γὰρ μόνῳ καὶ δίκαιοι φαίνεσθαι νόμοις εὐαγγελικοῖς ἐπετήδευον, ὄμματι προφητικῷ ὡς ἤδη τεθέντας προβλέποντες· καὶ τὸ, «Χεῖρα τὴν ἀριστερὰν τὸ δεξιὸν ἀποκρύπτειν,» πειρᾶσθαι πολίτευμα, φθάνειν τῆς τοιαύτης νομοθεσίας ἔργῳ καὶ πράξει τὸ πρόσταγμα· πρόσκειται δὲ καὶ τοῦτο τῇ ψήφῳ σαφῶς ἡ πασῶν ὁμοῦ τῶν Μωσαῖκῶν ἐντολῶν καὶ δικαιωμάτων ἄμεμπτος πλήρωσις, ἵνα δείξῃ τοὺς ταυτηνὶ τὴν ἔννομον ἔχοντας, ἑκατέρους ἑκατέροις, τοῖς τε τῆς παλαιᾶς νομοθεσίας στεφανουμένους αὐχήμασιν, καὶ τοῖς τῆς εὐαγγελικῆς πολιτείας λαμπρυνομένους ὑψώμασιν.

ιβʹ.Τοιούτους γὰρ ἔδει καὶ ἔπρεπε καὶ τοὺς Ἰωάννου προβολέας ὁρᾶσθαι· ἐπειδὴ καὶ ὁ ἐκ τούτων ἡμῖν Διαθήκης ἑκατέρας μεσίτης ἤμελλε δείκνυσθαι, καὶ συνέχειν ἐν ἑαυτῷ τῶν ἀμφοτέρων ἀληθῶς τὸ ἐξαίρετον· ὡμολόγηται γὰρ ὡς τῆς μὲν Πρεσβυτέρας τέλος ὡρίζετο (τοῦ γὰρ προφητικοῦ χοροῦ τὸν ἀριθμὸν αὐτὸς τετελείωκεν, ἐπειδὴ καὶ πάντες οἱ προφῆται ἕως Ἰωάννου τοῦ Βαπτιστοῦ προεφήτευσαν), τῆς δὲ Νεωτέρας ἀρχὴ καὶ προοίμιον· πάντων γὰρ τῶν ἀποστόλων αὐτὸς προηγήσατο· ἐπειδὴ προϊέναι τε καὶ προτρέχειν Χριστοῦ ἀξίαν ἁγίων ἀγγελικὴν ἀπεστέλλετο· Θεοῦ γὰρ ὡς ἀγγελος προβαδίζειν κεκέλευστο, «Καὶ ἀπὸ τῶν ἡμερῶν 3341Ἰωάννου τοῦ Βαπτιστοῦ ἡ βασιλεία τῶν οὐρανῶν βιάζεται, καὶ βιασταὶ ἁρπάζουσιν αὐτήν· ἐξ ἐκείνου γὰρ λαβοῦσα τὴν ἀρχὴν βιάζεσθαι διὰ τῆς εὐαγγελικῆς ἀγωγῆς ἑκουσίως ὑπὸ πάντων ἁρπάζεται· ἐξ· ἱερέως τοίνυν καὶ προφήτου πατρὸς καὶ τἄλλα λαμπροῦ καὶ θεόφρονος ἐν κοιλίᾳ μητρὸς ἱερᾶς καὶ προφήτιδος, Ἰωάννης οὗτος ὁ ἐν πᾶσι προφήταις ἔχων τὸ περισσὸν συλλαμβάνεται, ὃς καὶ μόνος ἐν γαστρὶ προεφήτευσεν· ἐπεὶ παρόντα γαστρὶ παρθενικῇ τὸν Δεσπότην ἥνωσε, καὶ μόνος ἐν γαστρὶ Πνεύματος ἁγίου γέγονεν ἔγκυος, κυοφορῶν ἐν ἑαυτῷ καὶ κυοφορούμενος· κυοφορούμενος μὲν στείρᾳ μητρὶ, κυοφορῶν δὲ τὴν χάριν τοῦ Πνεύματος καὶ ὠδῖνα προφητικὴν πρὶν ὠδινηθῆναι ποιούμενος, καὶ πρὸ τοῦ τεχθῆναι τίκτων τὸ Πνεύματος χάρισμα· εἴπερ ἐν γαστρὶ, κατὰ τὸν θεῖον Ἡσαΐαν, λαμβάνουσι καὶ ὠδίνουσι· καὶ πάλιν ἔπειτα τίκτουσιν οἱ προφητικῶς ὑπηρετοῦντες τῷ Πνεύματι, καὶ τὴν κρύφιον τούτου βουλὴν εἰς τοὐμφανὲς ἐξαγγέλλοντες· ἀλλὰ ταῦτα μὲν Ἰωάννης ὕστερον ἔδρασεν μετὰ τὴν αὐτοῦ παράδοξον σύλληψιν· ἐν στείρᾳ γὰρ μήτρᾳ
